# Engine coverage

The goal is a full MTG rules engine. This file is the honest state of it: what
the model actually implements, and what it does not. Scenario tests are written
against this list, so keep it current - a mechanic moves from "not modelled" to
"modelled" only when a scenario test covers it.

Read this before writing a scenario. If the mechanic is in the second table, the
test you write should be `xfail` with a reason, not a test bent until it passes.

## Modelled

| Area | Rules | Where | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| Card data | - | `models/card.py` | `test_card.py` |
| "See the top N, then optionally move one of them" - a whole family, not a card list | CR 701.17a, 701.17b, 701.17c, 401.2, 608.2d, 609.3, 110.4a, 122.1a, 207.2c, 207.2d | `_SELECT_FROM_TOP_RE` / `_select_from_top_runner` in `machines/effects.py`, dispatched on the ability side (`_parse_select_from_top_clause`), the spell side (`_parse_select_from_top_spell_clause`) and per mode (`parse_mode_effect`). One shape covers a **mill N** or a **look at the top N** opening, an optional "you may put a \[land\|creature\|permanent\|artifact\|enchantment\|instant\|sorcery\|planeswalker\] card from among them" into the hand, onto the battlefield (tapped or not), or into the graveyard, an "If you don't, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature" tail, a "You gain N life" tail, and a "Put the rest ..." tail. `_parse_look_top_card_conditional_clause` is its one-card cousin (Fecund Greenshell). Both halves are real questions: `Game.may` for CR 608.2d's "you may" and `DecisionKind.CHOOSE_CARDS` for *which* card - **no new `DecisionKind` was needed**. Six, Ostrich-Horse, Ainok Wayfarer, Scout the City's "Look Around" mode and Fecund Greenshell all read off this one template, none by name | `scenarios/test_select_from_the_top_of_your_library.py`, `scenarios/test_six.py` |
| Tokens: what one is, creating one, copying one | CR 111.1-111.4, 111.6, 111.10, 111.13, 701.7a, 707.2, 110.1, 205.2c, 202.3a | `models/token.py` | `scenarios/test_tokens.py`, `scenarios/test_role_tokens.py` |
| A token put onto the battlefield attacking | CR 508.4, 508.4a, 508.4c, 506.3a-c, 508.8 | `token._put_onto_the_battlefield_attacking`, which sets `Combat.attacked_this_combat` for CR 508.8's second clause ("or put onto the battlefield attacking") as well as appending the `Attack`, so such a creature's own declare blockers and combat damage steps are not skipped | `scenarios/test_tokens.py`, `scenarios/test_tokens_created_attacking.py`, `scenarios/judge/test_judge_put_onto_the_battlefield_attacking.py` |
| A token that has left the battlefield staying gone | CR 111.8 | `TokenState.move_to` refuses the move; `models/token.move_refused` is asked by `Player.add`/`Player.move` and by every zone helper in `models/game.py`, so the token's `zone` and the list it sits in agree | `scenarios/test_tokens.py` |
| A dead token ceasing to exist, dispatched | CR 704.5d, 111.7, 704.3 | `tokens_that_cease_to_exist`, called at the start of `Game._state_based_actions_once` | `scenarios/test_tokens.py`, `scenarios/test_role_tokens.py` |
| Replacing a token *being created* | CR 701.7b, 111.2, 614.5, 616.2 | `EventKind.CREATE_TOKENS` raised by `create_tokens`/`token_copy_of`, Doubling Season's token half in `machines/replacement.py` | `scenarios/test_token_creation_replacement.py` |
| An Aura on an illegal object, dispatched | CR 704.5m, 303.4c, 702.5a | `machines/keywords.aura_state_based_actions`, called from `Game._state_based_actions_once` before `equipment_state_based_actions`. Also judges an Aura attached to a *player* now - the attachment is read off `attachment` directly rather than through `attached_to`, which only ever narrows to a card | `scenarios/test_aura_attachment.py`, `scenarios/test_enchant.py` |
| A resolving Aura spell attaching to what it targeted | CR 608.3c, 303.4a, 303.4d, 701.3a | `Game.resolve_top` re-resolves the Aura's announced target through `_aura_attachment` (which is `resolve_targets`, so a target that changed identity or left is read fresh, not off a stale id) and passes it to `Game.enter_the_battlefield`'s new `attach_to` parameter, which attaches in the same event a Containment Priest-style redirect is applied in - not as a second mutation afterwards, so a redirect to exile never leaves the Aura attached to something that never arrived. Before this, every permanent spell went through `enter_the_battlefield` identically, an Aura landed with no attachment at all, and `aura_state_based_actions` correctly binned it a moment later - CR 704.5m was right, CR 608.3c's attach was simply never done. `CardState.attach_to` also self-attach-guards and, if `enter_the_battlefield` is ever asked to attach an Aura to itself anyway, routes it to its owner's graveyard (CR 303.4d) instead of raising out of a spell's resolution | `scenarios/test_aura_resolution.py` |
| Every Role but the newest | CR 704.5z, 303.7a, 613.7a | `Game._roles_beyond_the_newest`, reading `machines/layers.ContinuousEffects.entered` for the timestamp | `scenarios/test_role_tokens.py` |
| How much a prevention shield prevented | CR 615.5, 609.7b | `PreventionShield.prevented` and `gains_life_when_prevented`, set by `Game.apply_prevention_shield` | `scenarios/test_prevention_effects.py` |
| A keyword ability's effect on resolution | CR 608.2, 702.108a, 702.21a, 702.79a, 702.85a, 702.35a | `Game._effect_of` asks `machines/keywords.keyword_effect_for` before the card-name registries, so prowess, ward, persist, cascade, cycling, equip and madness resolve | `scenarios/test_prowess.py`, `test_ward.py`, `test_persist.py`, `test_cascade.py`, `test_madness.py` |
| Earthbend, a CR 701 keyword *action* embedded in another ability's effect text | CR 701.66a, 205.1b, 613.4b, 613.4c, 603.7a, 603.7e | `machines/effects._parse_earthbend_clause`/`earthbend`. Not printed with the word "target" - `machines/targeting.profile_for_text` is taught the keyword's own implied "target land you control" restriction so CR 601.2c's announcement still works. The land gains the creature type additively (`layers.add_type`, never `layers.set_basic_land_type`'s CR 305.7 path, which would strip its mana ability) and is set to 0/0 in sublayer 7b, with the `N` +1/+1 counters going on as real counters (`Game.add_counters`) rather than a second continuous effect, so CR 613.4's fixed sublayer order makes 0/0 + one counter unconditionally a 1/1 with no window where it is a legal-to-die 0/0. The animate/0-0/haste continuous effects are `Duration.INDEFINITE` and scoped with `layers.only_this_incarnation` to the exact pre-return incarnation of the land (by `zone_change_stamp`, not just `id`) - without that scoping the land would come back animated forever after its own delayed return, since `ContinuousEffects.end_the_turn` never clears an indefinite effect. "Dies or is put into exile" is represented as two `DelayedTrigger`s (`TriggerEvent.DIES`, and the new `TriggerEvent.LEAVES_BATTLEFIELD_TO_EXILE` now emitted by `Game.exile_permanent`, which previously emitted no event at all), returning tapped under the earthbend ability's own controller (CR 603.7e) rather than the owner-and-untapped default `delayed.return_subject_to_battlefield_under_owner` uses for a blink effect; whichever fires first drops its sibling. Fixed alongside it: `DelayedTrigger.matches` ignored `subject_id` entirely - harmless for every delayed trigger before this one, since they all watch `BEGINNING_OF_STEP`, whose event never carries a subject, but earthbend is the first event-keyed, subject-scoped delayed trigger and would otherwise have its one-shot `remaining` spent by an unrelated creature's death | `scenarios/test_earthbend.py` |
| A keyword line printed beside a spell's own resolution sentence | CR 702.1, CR 207.2a, CR 113.3 | `_strip_keyword_ability_lines` in `machines/effects.py` recognises a keyword-ability line by its **shape** and drops it before `_CLAUSE_SEPARATOR` ever splits the rest: a line carrying no full stop and no colon (`Flying`, `Improvise`, `Islandcycling {1}`, `Suspend 3—{2}{B}{B}`), or a keyword cost printed after an unspaced em dash (`Evoke—Exile a black card from your hand.`, `Flashback—Sacrifice a Mountain.`). A line containing " —" is never stripped, which is what keeps CR 700.2's modal header (`Choose one —`) and every CR 207.2c ability word out of it. Multi-line text only, so a single activated ability's own effect text is untouched. This **replaced a whitelist** of one hand-written regex per keyword (`_SUSPEND_LINE_RE`, `_CASCADE_LINE_RE`, `_REPLICATE_LINE_RE`, `_CYCLING_LINE_RE`, `_FLASHBACK_LINE_RE`, `_HARMONIZE_LINE_RE`, `_SNEAK_LINE_RE`, `_REBOUND_LINE_RE`, `_FUSE_LINE_RE`, `_STORM_LINE_RE`, `_SPLICE_LINE_RE`, `_CONVOKE_LINE_RE`, `_DELVE_LINE_RE`, `_CREW_LINE_RE`, `_KICKER_LINE_RE`), all fifteen of which are now deleted: the whitelist only ever worked for keywords somebody had already met, so improvise and typecycling had no entry and **Metallic Rebuke**, **Whir of Invention** and **Lórien Revealed** each lost their whole effect to a line nothing here executes | `scenarios/test_keyword_lines_do_not_silence_a_spell.py`, `scenarios/test_living_end.py`, `scenarios/test_cascade.py::test_violent_outbursts_own_pump_fails_closed_instead_of_silently` |
| "Each player \<sequence of actions>", read whole | CR 608.2c, 701.13a, 701.21a, 400.7, 110.2a | `_EACH_PLAYER_EXILE_SACRIFICE_RETURN_RE` and `each_player_exiles_sacrifices_and_returns_creatures` in `machines/effects.py`, matched against the whole (preamble-stripped) sentence in `_spell_effect_or_notice` ahead of the ordinary per-clause split, because CR 608.2c's own "then"s inside one sentence are not CR 608.2c's *own* clause boundaries the way `_CLAUSE_SEPARATOR`'s bare "then" reads them | `scenarios/test_living_end.py` |
| A duration-bearing group pump, told apart from CR 611.3's anthem | CR 611.2, 611.3 | `is_resolution_layer_effect_text` in `machines/layers.py` used to route *any* "Creatures you control get +N/+N..." opening to the anthem exemption regardless of what followed, so "Creatures you control get +1/+0 until end of turn." (Violent Outburst) never even reached the fail-closed check. An explicit duration can never be CR 611.3's forever-lasting static shape, so it no longer takes the anthem exemption. The row used to end "the actual continuous effect for a team-wide temporary pump is still not built (queued below)" and to cite a test named `test_violent_outbursts_own_pump_fails_closed_instead_of_silently`; both are stale. `_mass_pump_spell_effect` in `machines/effects.py` builds the real continuous effect now, and the test was renamed to match | `scenarios/test_cascade.py::test_violent_outbursts_own_pump_actually_pumps` |
| A discard that goes through the replacement effects | CR 701.9a, 702.35a, 614.1 | `machines/keywords.discard`, reached by `CostComponent.pay` and by `Player.discard` through the `Player.game` back-reference | `scenarios/test_madness.py` |
| Indestructible, enforced | CR 702.12b, 701.8a, 701.8b, 704.5f-h | `Game.destroy` refuses it; `Game._dies_to_state_based_action` drops CR 704.5g and CR 704.5h and keeps CR 704.5f, which CR 701.8b says is not destruction | `scenarios/test_indestructible.py` |
| A cost sentence no `CostKind` fits, activated | CR 602.1a, 701.43a, 118.3 | `Game.activation_cost` routes `CostKind.OTHER` to `machines/cost.cost_component_for_phrase` and fills in the source permanent for a `this` phrase | `scenarios/test_exert.py` |
| The untap symbol `{Q}` as an activation cost | CR 107.6, 602.5a, 118.3 | Half of it. `machines/abilities._classify_cost` reads `{Q}` as `CostKind.UNTAP`, `Game.begin_activating` already gates CR 602.5a on it beside `{T}`, and `machines/cost` now has `ComponentKind.UNTAP` (`untap_cost`, `_PHRASE_UNTAP_SYMBOL`) with CR 107.6's "a permanent that's already untapped can't be untapped again to pay the cost" in `_why_not_untap` and a re-tap rewind (CR 733.1). `Game.activation_cost` now has the matching `CostKind.UNTAP` branch (`untap_cost(permanent, ...)`, CR 107.6's "this permanent"), so the whole cost sequence pays. Umbral Mantle's granted `{3}, {Q}` is the pool's only printing, and its *effect* works too now: `machines/layers.effects_for` reads a granted ability's own text off `CardState.granted_ability_texts` (kept in step by `sync_granted_ability_texts`), so the +2/+2 applies without a `LAYER_EFFECTS` row. This row used to end "the +2/+2 never applies" and cite an `xfail` in `judge/test_judge_costs.py`; both were stale - that test passes, and asserts the pump | `judge/test_judge_costs.py`, `scenarios/test_attached_creature_statics.py` |
| Role tokens and what they grant | CR 111.10k, 303.7 | `LAYER_EFFECTS["Monster"]` in `machines/layers.py` | `scenarios/test_role_tokens.py` |
| Zones | CR 400 | `models/zone.py` | `test_zone.py` |
| Outside the game, and bringing a card in from it | CR 400.11, 400.11a, 400.11b, 400.11c, 100.4, 100.4a, 108.3b, 103.2a | **Not an eighth zone.** CR 400.1 numbers seven zones and outside the game is not among them; CR 400.11a says only that "cards in a player's sideboard are outside the game", so `Zone.SIDEBOARD` stays the *storage* for those objects and `Zone.is_outside_the_game` / `Player.outside_the_game` are the rules-level reading of it. `Player.owned_outside_the_game` filters by CR 108.3b's **owner** (not controller), and `Player.bring_into_the_game` is CR 400.11b's one-way move, refusing a card that is not outside the game and a card the player does not own. Deck legality is untouched by a fetch: `Deck.check_legality` counts the registered decklist (CR 100.2a, 100.4a), not the physical sideboard, and `Match._fresh` reloads the whole list next game, which is CR 400.11b's "until the game ends" bound. Reachable only by an ability CR 400.11c's exception allows - Karn, the Great Creator's `−2` is the pool's one such card | `scenarios/test_outside_the_game.py` |
| Revealing a card | CR 701.20a, 701.20b, 701.20c | `Game.reveal` appends one `Reveal` per card actually shown and moves nothing (CR 701.20b). Public information is the *reveal*, not the group it came from: a card revealed out of a sideboard nobody may look at makes exactly one card public. `Game.reveals` is the whole record; there is no "revealed zone" | `scenarios/test_outside_the_game.py` |
| Face-up versus face-down exile, as a choosability question | CR 406.3, 406.3a | `Game.face_up_cards_in_exile(owner, matches)`. A card exiled face down "can't be examined by any player", so it can never answer "a face-up artifact card you own in exile". **CR 406.3a's other half is modelled too**: `CardState.has_no_characteristics` blanks name, types, subtypes, supertypes, colors, keywords, text, mana cost and power/toughness for a card face down in exile, so CR 202.3a reads its mana value as 0 (DDR#956's face-down Emrakul contributing nothing to "the greatest mana value among cards in exile"). Scoped to a card in **no pile that grants permission to play it** (`exile_annotation is None`): CR 601.3f lets a player who may look at a face-down exiled card cast it, and CR 406.3a turns it face up before announcement - this engine does not turn it face up at announcement (a `machines/casting.py` change), so a foretold, plotted, hideaway, madness or suspended card stays readable while it waits | `scenarios/test_outside_the_game.py` |
| A static ability that prohibits activating another permanent's abilities | CR 602.5, 611.1, 611.3, 611.3a, 611.3b, 604.2, 605.1a | Not a layer effect: CR 611.1's third case is a continuous effect that "affects players or **the rules of the game**", which writes no characteristic, so `machines/layers.py` has nothing to compute and the prohibition is felt at `Game.why_cannot_activate` instead (CR 602.5). `_parse_activation_prohibition` reads "Activated abilities of \<card types> \[your opponents control] can't be activated \[unless they're mana abilities]" off a battlefield permanent's own static line, re-read on every query (CR 611.3a: not locked in) and gone the moment its source leaves (CR 611.3b). "Your opponents control" is resolved against the **source's** controller, so Karn, the Great Creator does not stop his own controller's artifacts, while the symmetric printings (Collector Ouphe, Clarion Conqueror) do. The affected permanent's card types are read off the computed board (CR 613.5), so an artifact Karn's own `+1` animated is still prohibited. A prohibition sentence in another shape records a `LayerNotice` and blocks nothing - except Pithing Needle's and Disruptor Flute's "sources with the chosen name", which already have an `UnwiredChoiceNotice` and are skipped rather than counted twice | `scenarios/test_outside_the_game.py` |
| A continuous effect that restricts or loosens *casting*, rather than activating | CR 611.1, 611.3, 611.3a, 611.3b, 602.1a, 800.4m | **W5.** The casting-side twin of the activation-prohibition row above, in a new small module rather than a bigger one: `machines/casting_restrictions.py`. Two shapes, told apart by whether a live battlefield permanent can answer for them. **A live static** - Teferi, Time Raveler's "Each opponent can cast spells only any time they could cast a sorcery." - is read fresh off the battlefield on every query by `sorcery_speed_restriction_source`, exactly like `_parse_activation_prohibition` above, and dies the instant Teferi does. **A recorded `CastingRuleEffect`** covers the two shapes a live read cannot answer for: Ranger-Captain of Eos's "Sacrifice this creature: Your opponents can't cast noncreature spells this turn" is created when the ability *resolves*, by which point the sacrifice (its own cost, CR 602.1a) has already sent the source to the graveyard, so nothing is left to scan; and Teferi's own `+1`, "Until your next turn, you may cast sorcery spells as though they had flash," is a temporary grant to its controller alone, reusing `Duration.UNTIL_YOUR_NEXT_TURN` (CR 800.4m) rather than `machines.casting.PlayPermission`, which names one specific card and is the wrong shape for a blanket permission over every sorcery cast. Both recorded effects are dropped by `Game.casting_rule_effects` list rewrites - `cleanup_step` for the "this turn" restriction (CR 514.2) and `begin_turn_for` for the "until your next turn" grant (CR 800.4m), mirroring how `play_permissions` already drops its own two durations. Read from two sites: `machines.casting.why_cannot_cast_now` (the static, folded into the CR 117.1a timing question, checked *before* the ordinary instant/flash exceptions so a restricted opponent loses them, but *after* `can_cast_sorcery` so a legally-timed cast needs no exception at all) and the new `why_cannot_cast_restriction` (the flat noncreature ban, unrelated to timing). Both are asked only once, in `Casting.model_post_init`, as a `Casting` is created - never re-asked of one already on the stack, so a restriction that appears mid-response does not reach back and unmake an announcement that already finished (CR 601.6). **W15 adds three more live statics and one spell-side quality**, all in the same module (CR 601.3 is the rule each expresses, CR 101.2 the precedence). (1) **A turn window** - Voice of Victory's "Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn", combining "your opponents" with CR 102.1's active player; (2) **a count** - High Noon's "Each player can't cast more than one spell each turn", counted off `Game.spells_cast_this_turn` (which already excludes copies, CR 707.10). Both ride the reader `machines.casting.why_cannot_cast_restriction` already calls, renamed `blanket_cant_cast_restriction` with `targeted_cant_cast_restriction` kept as the call site's alias, so **both are enforced end to end** with no change to `machines/casting.py`. (3) **An origin zone** - Grafdigger's Cage's "Players can't cast spells from graveyards or libraries" and Soulless Jailer's "Players can't cast noncreature spells from graveyards or exile", read by `zone_cast_prohibition_source` (spell + origin zone; "players" is everyone, controller included). **Now enforced end to end**: `machines.casting.why_cannot_cast_restriction` asks `zone_cast_prohibition_source` about `spell.zone` - the zone the card is in when the announcement is made, which is the origin zone - so the Cage refuses a flashback announcement outright (CR 601.3's "no rule or effect prohibits", CR 101.2 beating flashback's own permission). Note this is *not* CR 113.6f, which is about an ability restricting **its own** object's zones (flashback), not every other object's. Cage's other half, "Creature cards in graveyards and libraries can't enter the battlefield", is a zone-change prohibition and belongs to `machines/replacement.py`, where it is **now built** as a CR 614.17d can't-enter effect (`_cant_enter_replacements`) that returns the card to its origin zone - see the "Effects that say something *can't* happen" row below. (4) **`cannot_be_countered`** - Supreme Verdict's "This spell can't be countered", CR 113.6g ("functions on the stack") and CR 604.5. It is **not** a targeting restriction: CR 608.2b knows nothing about counterability, so the Counterspell is announced legally and resolves, and what must fail is CR 701.6a's verb inside `Game.counter` (`models/game.py`) reached via `counter_targets` (`machines/effects.py`). It is **enforced end to end now**: `Game.counter` returns the spell untouched and emits no counter event when `cannot_be_countered` says so, guarded *above* the `to_exile` branch so an exile rider (Force of Negation, Delay) cannot exile what it could not counter | `scenarios/test_casting_restrictions.py`, `scenarios/test_high_noon.py`, `scenarios/test_voice_of_victory.py`, `scenarios/test_zone_cast_prohibitions.py`, `scenarios/test_cant_be_countered.py` |
| Turn structure | CR 500-514 | `machines/turn.py` | `test_turn.py`, `scenarios/test_turn_cycle.py` |
| Priority | CR 117 | `machines/priority.py` | `scenarios/test_priority.py` |
| The steps in which **nobody** receives priority | CR 500.3, 502.4, 514.3, 514.3a, 117.3a | `PriorityMachine.withheld` holds the reason nobody has priority, so `PriorityMachine.holder` and `Game.priority_holder` are `None` rather than naming a player who may not act, and `Game.pass_priority` refuses with the rule's own words. `Game.sync_priority_with_step` withholds and restores it at every point the step pointer moves; `Game.step_grants_priority` is the live answer, and it is the one to ask - `TurnMachine.grants_priority` answers for the shape of the step only. Untap is unconditional (CR 502.4) and holds any ability that triggers in it in `pending_triggers` until the next step (CR 502.4's second sentence, CR 603.3). Cleanup is CR 514.3's "normally": a cleanup step with triggers waiting or something on the stack is CR 514.3a's exception and does grant priority. **Not modelled**: CR 514.3a beyond its first sentence, which `knowledge_search` could not return - in particular whether a further cleanup step then begins | `scenarios/test_steps_without_priority.py`, `scenarios/judge/test_judge_cleanup_discard.py` |
| `Game.advance` refusing to leave a step while the stack is non-empty, and authorizing who may call it | CR 500.2, 500.3, 117.4, 405.5, 117.3a, 723.5 | `Game.advance` raises rather than crossing a step boundary while `Game.step_grants_priority` is true and the stack is non-empty (the untap/cleanup exception is CR 500.3's), and raises when `player` is neither the active player nor their CR 723.5 controller | `scenarios/test_advancing_the_turn.py` |
| Casting announcement | CR 601.2 | `machines/casting.py` | `test_casting.py` |
| **CR 601.2a: the card moves to the stack as the *first* step of proposing a cast** | CR 601.2a, 112.1, 405.1, 601.6, 733.1, 115.5, 400.7, 400.7g, 400.7h, 406.5 | `Casting.model_post_init` performs the move ("To propose the casting of a spell, a player first moves that card ... from where it is to the stack. It becomes the topmost object on the stack"), five steps before CR 601.2f prices the spell - so a spell that counts its controller's graveyard no longer counts itself (DDR#996's flashed-back Lesson and Gran-Gran's three-Lesson gate). The card is lifted out of whatever list held it, player zones and shared zones alike. Because CR 400.7 makes the object on the stack a *new* object, the zone, identity stamp and exile annotation it had are recorded on the `Casting` and every announcement-time question - a keyword's own zone clause (CR 604.6), a `PlayPermission`'s `names` check (kept alive across the move by CR 400.7h), the static free-cast grant's zone - is asked of that recorded view (`Casting._as_announced`) rather than of where the card is sitting now. Timing questions that read "the stack is empty" (CR 307.5, plot's CR 702.170a) are asked with the spell momentarily off the stack list (`_as_of_the_proposal`), because those are conditions on *beginning* to cast, before the move. A refused announcement rewinds itself (CR 601.6, CR 733.1): `Casting.cancel` puts the card back with the stamp and pile it had, and an illegal target now cancels the whole proposal rather than leaving a half-announced spell on the stack. CR 115.5 ("A spell or ability on the stack is an illegal target for itself") became load-bearing at the same moment and is checked in `choose_targets` | `scenarios/test_announcement_moves_the_card_to_the_stack.py`, `judge/test_judge_illegal_play_win.py`, `judge/test_judge_costs.py` |
| **A spell's own cost reduction, charged on every cast** | CR 601.2f, 118.7a, 118.9d, 601.8 | `Casting.total_cost` folds in `Game.cost_reduction_for` and `Casting.determine_cost` locks the value in (`Casting.self_reduction`). Measured before the fix: Leyline Binding with all five basic land types out asked `{5}{W}` through `begin_paying()` while `Game.determine_total_cost` - an opt-in call no ordinary cast makes - answered `{W}` on the same board. Every self-discount shape `cost_reduction_for` reads (domain, Emrakul's card types, Hollow One's discards, Obsidian Charmaw, affinity, Mystical Dispute's announced target) was therefore computed and never charged, while cost *increases* already applied. The lock is CR 601.2f's own ("Then the resulting total cost becomes 'locked in'"), so a graveyard that grows after the cost was determined is worth nothing; `Game.determine_total_cost`'s own append is de-duplicated rather than stacked | `scenarios/test_self_reduction_reaches_the_cast.py`, `scenarios/test_cost_reduction_shapes.py`, `scenarios/test_dynamic_cost_reduction.py` |
| **An alternative cost printed as a plain, conditional sentence** | CR 118.9, 118.9a, 118.9b, 118.5, 604.6 | `conditional_alternative_cost_offers` in `machines/casting.py` reads Mindbreak Trap's "If an opponent cast three or more spells this turn, you may pay {0} rather than pay this spell's mana cost." - `machines/cost.py`'s own reader is anchored at "You may" and at Force of Negation's timing gate, and the condition here is a question about the game's history that only a machine holding the game can answer. Fails closed twice: an unreadable condition and an unmet one both leave the option listed with its reason rather than granting it. "pay {0}" is read as mana by stripping the verb (CR 118.5: {0} is a real cost that must be paid) | `scenarios/test_conditional_alternative_costs.py`, `judge/test_judge_mindbreak_trap.py` |
| **A keyword another permanent grants a spell as it is cast** | CR 610.5, 604.2, 110.4b, 205.2b | `granted_cast_options`/`_grant_as_you_cast` in `machines/casting.py`, offered by `Casting.use_keyword`, `Casting.cast_options` and `Casting.alternative_costs` alongside the card's own printed keywords. Ashling, the Limitless's "Elemental permanent spells you cast from your hand gain evoke {4} as you cast them" - CR 610.5's own shape, "These effects begin to apply to appropriate spells at the time the player proposes the casting of that spell", which is why it is read at announcement rather than off the card. The keyword is expanded by the same `_option_from_keyword` a printed one goes through, so a granted evoke costs what evoke costs (CR 702.74a). Evoke's *triggered* half reaches a granted keyword too: `Casting._use_keyword` stamps the announced keyword line onto `CardState.granted_ability_texts` (`KeywordCastOption.granted_line`), `machines/keywords.granted_keyword_triggered_abilities` writes CR 702.1's expansion out of it, and `machines/triggers.granted_triggered_abilities_of` is appended to the printed list inside `triggers_for` - so CR 400.7b/611.3d's "killing Ashling does not save the Elemental" holds, off the object rather than off the grantor | `scenarios/test_granted_cast_keywords.py`, `judge/test_judge_granted_evoke.py` |
| **A castability keyword granted to a card where it lies** | CR 611.3d, 400.7g, 113.6f, 702.34a | `_grant_on_a_card_in_a_zone` in `machines/casting.py`: Iroh, Grand Lotus's "During your turn, each Lesson card in your graveyard has flashback {1}." and its non-Lesson twin whose cost is "equal to that card's mana cost". CR 611.3d is the grant ("Continuous effects from static abilities ... may grant an ability to a permanent spell or card that allows it to be cast"), CR 400.7g keeps it applying once CR 601.2a has moved the card to the stack, and the granted keyword is what names the zone (CR 113.6f). The noun phrase is read as a conjunction of subtype/supertype words over a *disjunction* of card types ("non-Lesson instant and sorcery card"), with `non-` negating, and any word it cannot place drops the grant entirely. `why_cannot_cast_from` and `why_cannot_cast_now` both consult these grants, so the graveyard becomes a legal origin zone only while the granting permanent's condition holds | `scenarios/test_granted_cast_keywords.py`, `judge/test_judge_costs.py` |

| A card with no printed mana cost, refused unless an alternative cost applies | CR 118.6, 118.6a, 601.6 | `Casting._refuse_unless_mana_cost_payable` in `machines/casting.py`, called from `determine_cost` (CR 601.2f) before a payment ever opens. Found live: a real game log (3cce87b1a72b4442a7c1faad8cd51ba0) showed Living End - `Card.mana_cost == ""`, no mana symbols at all - cast straight from hand for an empty cost with no black source in play. `Casting.base_cost` parsed the blank string to a `ManaCost` with **zero symbols**, which `total_cost` then treated exactly like a genuinely printed `{0}` (CR 118.5a: one generic symbol worth zero, `ManaCost.parse("{0}").symbols` non-empty) - the engine had no way to tell "no cost printed" apart from "a cost of nothing", even though only the first is CR 118.6's unpayable cost. The check is general to every card whose printed mana cost carries no symbols, not special-cased to Living End, and steps aside the moment `Casting.alternative_cost` is set (CR 118.6a: an alternative cost, including "without paying its mana cost", makes an unpayable cost payable) - which is what lets Suspend's own free cast, or Cascade's free cast, or any other CR 118.9 alternative-cost path reach the same card. | `scenarios/test_suspend.py` |
| A keyword cost printed after an em dash | CR 702.1a | `KeywordAbility` in `machines/abilities.py` reads `Evoke—Exile a black card from your hand.`, `Suspend 3—{2}{B}{B}` | `scenarios/test_keyword_cost_forms.py` |
| A printed cost sentence turned into something payable | CR 118.3 | `cost_component_for_phrase`/`total_cost_for_phrase` and `CardRequirement` in `machines/cost.py` | `scenarios/test_keyword_cost_forms.py`, `scenarios/test_evoke.py` |
| Which zone a keyword lets a card be cast from | CR 601.3, 601.2b, 702.34a, 702.187a-b, 702.170d, 702.143a, 702.35a, 604.6, 611.3d, 610.5, 701.65a | `KeywordCastOption`, `keyword_cast_options`, `marker_cast_options`, `why_cannot_cast_from` in `machines/casting.py`. **The zone permission and the keyword's own cost are one clause, not two** - CR 702.34a's own wording is the clearest: `"Flashback [cost]" means "You may cast this card from your graveyard ... by paying [cost] rather than paying its mana cost"` - a single sentence, so the graveyard permission does not exist apart from the cost that replaces the mana cost. Flashback, mayhem (CR 702.187a-b), plot (CR 702.170d), foretell (CR 702.143a), madness (CR 702.35a), a card's own printed CR 604.6 zone permission, and a CR 610.5/611.3d granted keyword option are all this shape. Found live: a real game charged Faithful Mending (mana cost `{W}{U}`, `Flashback {1}{W}{U}`) its face cost while casting it from the graveyard with no keyword announced, because `_refuse_unless_allowed_from_its_zone` asked only `why_cannot_cast_from` - CR 601.3's "could *anything* allow this cast from here" - and treated a `KeywordCastOption`'s bare *existence* for that zone as the announcement. CR 601.2b's own text is why that is wrong: choosing to pay a keyword's alternative cost is a **previously made choice** the player announces, and CR 118.9a lets only one alternative cost apply to a spell, which presupposes a choice rather than the engine picking whichever option happens to exist. Fixed by asking a second question inside `_refuse_unless_allowed_from_its_zone`: for a non-hand origin, `Casting.keyword_used`/`.permission_used` must actually be set (or a live `PlayPermission` must already answer question one on its own - `usable_play_permission`, kept as a leniency so Ugin's `−11`, Ragavan's trigger, cascade and suspend keep working without an explicit `use_permission()` call in live play). The refusal names the claimable options the way `use_alternative_cost` already does ("name it: ..."), built off `Casting.cast_options` - never off a table of card names. **Evoke (CR 702.74a), dash (CR 702.109a), morph/megamorph/disguise (CR 702.37a, 702.37c), bestow (CR 702.103a's "you may choose"), and warp's hand half (CR 702.185a) are the deliberate exceptions**: each is a bare CR 118.9 alternative cost from the **hand**, which is already legal to cast from (CR 302.1) - `_refuse_unless_allowed_from_its_zone` returns immediately for `origin_zone is Zone.HAND` and never asks the second question of them. Warp's exile half (CR 702.185b) and CR 715.3d's Adventure are zone permissions too, and still need `keyword_used` set, even though their own cost is `pays_mana_cost=True` (the printed mana cost is the *right* cost there, not an undercharge). **Airbend (CR 701.65a)** was broken the opposite way and is now fixed alongside this: its `{2}` permission lives only in `marker_cast_options`, which `why_cannot_cast_from` never consulted, so a correctly-announced airbent cast (`use_keyword("Airbend")`, cost correctly `{2}`) was refused outright ("nothing allows ... (CR 601.3)"); `why_cannot_cast_from` now folds `marker_cast_options` into its option list alongside the printed and granted ones. | `scenarios/test_flashback.py`, `scenarios/test_mayhem.py`, `scenarios/test_plot.py`, `scenarios/test_foretell.py`, `scenarios/test_madness.py`, `scenarios/test_granted_cast_keywords.py`, `scenarios/test_warp.py`, `scenarios/test_adventure.py`, `scenarios/judge/test_judge_bending.py` |
| Suspend | CR 702.62a-d, 116.2f | Built as its three abilities, general to the keyword's own printed "Suspend N—[cost]" line (`KeywordAbility.count`/`.cost`), not special-cased to any one card. **The first**, CR 116.2f's special action - "if you could begin to cast this card ... you may pay [cost] and exile it with N time counters on it" - is `MarkedExile.suspend` in `machines/casting.py`, mirroring the already-modelled `MarkedExile.plot`/`.foretell`; gated by `why_cannot_cast_now`/`why_cannot_cast_restriction` (CR 702.62c's "take into consideration any effects that would prohibit that card from being cast") and deliberately *not* by CR 118.6 - CR 118.6 itself says attempting to cast an unpayable-cost card is legal, only paying it is not, which is why a card with no mana cost (Living End) can still be suspended. **The second**, the upkeep trigger removing one time counter, is `remove_suspend_time_counter`; not dispatched automatically, the same hand-driven shape this engine already gives every other delayed or off-stack trigger (`exile_warped`, combat's own declarations) - see `docs/scenario-tests.md`. **The third**, the last-counter trigger, is `finish_suspend`, whose "you" is the card's **owner** (CR 109.5's "or its owner (if it has no controller)", and a card in exile has no controller; CR 108.3 defines the owner) - not whoever performed the exile, which is what `ExileAnnotation.owner` records and what this used to read. It hands out a `PlayPermission` (CR 601.3's dynamic grant, the same primitive Ugin's -11 and Ragavan's trigger already use) rather than a new `DecisionKind` - the "you may play it" is left to the caller exactly the way Cascade's own "you may cast it" already is, so the caller's choice **is** whether it ever calls `Casting.use_permission()` on the permission handed back; not calling it is CR 702.62a's own "if you don't, it remains exiled." **This grant is `immediate=True` (`PlayPermission.immediate`), not the `until_end_of_turn=True` default a first pass left unoverridden.** CR 702.62a's third ability prints no "until end of turn" the way Ugin's `−11` and Ragavan's trigger both do; it is instead CR 117.2e's "resolving spells and abilities may instruct players to make choices or take actions... no player has priority while [it] is resolving" shape, so the chance to cast it exists only for as long as that instruction is being carried out - `immediate` scopes the grant to `granted_step` (the exact `TurnMachine.step` it fired in) rather than "the rest of the turn", and `why_cannot_cast_now` carries a matching exception (`usable_play_permission`) so a suspended sorcery can still be cast the moment the trigger resolves even during an upkeep step, which is never anyone's main phase - CR 117.1a's own restriction only governs casting through the ordinary priority system, and CR 117.1's preamble excludes exactly this "a spell or ability is instructing a player" case from it. Found live: a first pass modelled this permission with the plain `until_end_of_turn=True` default, which let the free cast wait all the way to a later main phase in the same turn and required a wrongly-reasoned "same as any other cast" timing gate in the process - `scenarios/test_suspend.py::test_the_free_cast_window_does_not_outlive_the_upkeep_trigger` pins the fix: the window is open in the upkeep step the trigger fired in and closed one step later, in the same turn. The creature-spell haste clause is `grant_suspend_haste`, applied the same way Postmortem Lunge's own no-duration-stated haste already is (`Duration.INDEFINITE`, `only_this_incarnation`-scoped) - "until you lose control of the spell or the permanent it becomes" is approximated by that scoping rather than separately timed to a control change. `Counter.TIME` and `ExileMarker.SUSPENDED` are new. Proved general against two real printings: Living End (Sorcery, Suspend 3—{2}{B}{B}, no mana cost) and Greater Gargadon (Creature, Suspend 10—{R}, its own printed {9}{R}), the latter also proving the haste grant actually reaches the layer engine. Still not modelled: an effect that *grants* suspend to a foreign card rather than reading it off the card's own text (Delay's counterspell - see the Not-modelled table) and CR 116.2f's `{X}`/107.3d variable suspend cost (no printing in the pool has one). | `scenarios/test_suspend.py` |
| An effect's **time-boxed permission to play specific exiled cards** | CR 601.3, 601.1a, 701.5b, 701.18a, 701.18b, 118.9, 118.9c, 107.3b, 514.2, 400.7, 108.3 | `PlayAction`/`PlayPermission`/`play_permissions_on`/`usable_play_permission` in `machines/casting.py`, `Game.grant_play_permission`/`Game.play_permission_for`/`Game.play_permissions`, announced on a cast with `Casting.use_permission` and enforced by `Casting._refuse_unless_allowed_from_its_zone` (CR 601.3) and by `Game.play_land`'s own zone check (CR 305.1, 701.18a). The dynamic twin of `KeywordCastOption`: that one is a *static* grant read off a card's own printed keyword, this is one a **resolving ability** hands out. Deliberately per object rather than per zone - a blanket "exile is castable" would let a player cast anything ever exiled, including an opponent's. Keyed by `card_id` **and** `zone_change_stamp`, so CR 400.7's new object loses it; `player` is who may play it, which CR 108.3 lets differ from the owner; `action` keeps CR 701.5b's *cast* apart from CR 701.18b's wider *play*, so only a `PLAY` permission reaches the land drop and it still costs CR 305.2a's one-per-turn allowance; `pays_mana_cost=False` is CR 118.9's "without paying its mana cost", announced separately (CR 118.9b) so "may cast this" and "may cast this free" stay two different grants. Timing is untouched for an *ordinary* grant - a sorcery under a permission with an explicit "until end of turn" printed (Ugin's `−11`, Ragavan's trigger) is still refused outside a main phase (CR 117.1a), because that cast goes through the normal priority system later. `immediate=True` (`PlayPermission.immediate`, `granted_step`) is the other shape, for a grant with no stated duration - CR 117.2e's own "instructed by a resolving ability, no priority in between" - and *does* let a sorcery through outside a main phase, but only for the exact step the grant was made in; see the Suspend row for why. Dropped in the cleanup step (CR 514.2), with `granted_turns_taken` as the backstop for a game driven step by step that never reaches one. | `scenarios/test_play_from_exile.py`, `scenarios/test_suspend.py` |
| Reading "[exile some cards]. Until end of turn, you may cast/play them." off printed text | CR 608.2c, 607.1, 107.1c, 701.23b, 601.3 | `_parse_exile_then_play_permission_pair` in `machines/effects.py`, with `_parse_exile_antecedent`'s two shapes ("exile the top card of that player's library" - Ragavan; "search your library for any number of `<quality>` cards, exile them, then shuffle" - Ugin's −11) and `_PLAY_PERMISSION_RE`'s permission sentence. Read as one CR 608.2c instruction, never two, because the pronoun is CR 607.1's anaphora back to what the first clause exiled; number has to agree ("that card" is not "those cards"), and an unreadable stated quality refuses the whole pair rather than searching for something it cannot describe. "Any number" is CR 107.1c's zero-or-more, asked card by card through `Game.search_library(optional=True)` (CR 701.23b), so finding nothing is legal and grants nothing. `_named_type_matcher` now reads a "non`<type>`" quality word (CR 109.2), which is what "colorless nonland" needs. | `scenarios/test_play_from_exile.py` |
| "Add `<mana>`" as the resolution of an ability that is **not** a mana ability | CR 605.1a, 605.5, 606.2, 602.2, 113.3b, 106.4 | `_parse_add_mana_clause` in `machines/effects.py`. Ugin, Eye of the Storms' `0: Add {C}{C}{C}.` is a loyalty ability (CR 606.2: a loyalty symbol in the cost), and CR 605.1a's criteria include "it's not a loyalty ability" - so CR 605.3b's "doesn't go on the stack" is false of it. `Game.activate_ability` already refused it the mana-ability fast path; nothing resolved it afterwards, so the mana silently never arrived. It does now, at resolution, into the controller's pool (CR 106.4). | `scenarios/test_loyalty_abilities.py` |

Its intervening *if* is answered by `intervening_if_holds` in `machines/triggers.py` from `CastRecord.keyword` (CR 118.9a allows one alternative cost, and `Casting.cast` records which was announced), so a hardcast incarnation's ability does not trigger at all and one that was never cast answers False rather than failing closed. Not a replacement effect and not a delayed trigger: the creature enters, its own enters triggers happen alongside this one, and the sacrifice is CR 701.21a's, so it dies and death triggers see it. | Mayhem: casting from the graveyard the card you discarded this turn | CR 702.187a, 702.187b, 701.9a | `_option_from_keyword`'s mayhem branch in `machines/casting.py` - the flashback shape (graveyard zone, alternative cost replacing the mana cost) plus `KeywordCastOption.discarded_this_turn`, the one condition flashback has no equivalent of. It is a fact about *this card* and about *who* discarded it, which `Game.cards_discarded_this_turn`'s per-player count cannot answer, so `machines/keywords.discard` records `CardState.discarded_by`/`discarded_on_turn` on the card the far side of the move, `CardState.move_to` clears both the moment it leaves the graveyard (CR 400.7), and `Casting` carries them across CR 601.2a's move to the stack the same way it already carries the exile annotation. CR 702.187c's costless "Mayhem" (which says *play*, not cast) is deliberately unread - no printing in the pool has it | `judge/test_judge_cleanup_discard.py` |
| Airbend: the {2} cast an airbent card's exile pile grants | CR 701.65a | Whole now, cast included. `ExileMarker.AIRBENT` and `machines/casting.marker_cast_options` are the permission - a cast option read off the *pile* rather than off any printed keyword, since airbend is a CR 701 keyword action and prints nothing on what it moves. `{2}` replaces the mana cost (CR 118.9), the option is the **owner's** (`ExileAnnotation.owner`, and the airbender is usually somebody else), and there is no `later_turn` because CR 701.65a states none. The exile itself is built too: `machines/effects._parse_airbend_clause` performs CR 701.65a's "that player exiles those objects" through `Game.exile_cards` (one instruction, CR 603.2c) and writes the `ExileMarker.AIRBENT` note onto each exiled **card**. A token gets no note - CR 701.65a's permission is "for each *card*" and CR 111.6 says a token is not one - and CR 111.7/704.5d have it cease to exist before anybody could use one anyway. Reaching a battlefield creature at all needed `machines/targeting.py`: "target creature or spell" was read as one conjunction and came back stack-only, so the new `TargetRequirement.or_any_spell` keeps the two halves of that noun list apart (CR 112.1). **Found live and fixed**: `why_cannot_cast_from` (CR 601.3's existence question) never consulted `marker_cast_options` at all - only a printed `KeywordCastOption` or a live `PlayPermission` could satisfy it - so an airbent card correctly announced with `use_keyword("Airbend")` (cost correctly `{2}`) was still refused outright by `_refuse_unless_allowed_from_its_zone` with "nothing allows ... (CR 601.3)"; an airbent card could not be cast **at all**. `why_cannot_cast_from` now folds `marker_cast_options` (filtered to the zone in question) into its option list alongside the printed and granted ones - see the "Which zone a keyword lets a card be cast from" row above, which this shares its fix with. CR 701.65b's "triggers whenever a player airbends" has no printing in the pool and emits nothing | `judge/test_judge_bending.py` |
| Bestow: the Aura a creature spell becomes | CR 702.103a, 702.103b, 702.103f, 604.6 | Most of it. `_option_from_keyword`'s bestow branch is CR 702.103a's alternative cost from the hand; `_printed_zone_permissions` reads a card's own CR 604.6 sentence ("You may cast this card from your graveyard using its bestow ability") and copies the keyword's option to the zone it names, same cost and all; `CardState.bestowed` is CR 702.103b - while it is set and the object is on the stack or the battlefield the object is an Aura enchantment, not a creature, and has enchant creature - carried across the stack-to-battlefield move only, and cleared by `unattach` (CR 702.103f). The *cost* is read now too: `machines/cost.ComponentKind.COLLECT_EVIDENCE` (built by `collect_evidence_cost`, matched by `_PHRASE_COLLECT_EVIDENCE`) is CR 701.59a's "exile any number of cards from your graveyard with total mana value N or greater", so Detective's Phoenix's `Bestow—{R}, Collect evidence 6` reads as a whole `TotalCost`; `_why_not_collect_evidence` refuses a graveyard that cannot reach N (CR 701.59b, CR 118.3) and `_evidence_to_exile` takes the fewest cards that do. CR 702.103b's *target* comes from `Casting._with_bestowed_target`, because the printed card is neither an Aura nor carries enchant, so `targeting._with_aura_target` cannot reach it. Missing: the resolution half - a bestowed spell resolves onto the battlefield unattached and with `bestowed` cleared, so nothing is enchanted and the Aura's own "+2/+2 and has flying and haste" never applies | `scenarios/test_granted_cast_keywords.py` |

| A triggered ability's controller is **asked** for its targets | CR 115.1, 601.2c-d, 603.3d, 608.2d | `DecisionKind.CHOOSE_TARGETS`; `Game.put_triggers_on_stack` → `Game._ask_trigger_targets` asks each ability *instance* through `Game.ask`, addressed to `ability.controller`, before it is appended to the stack; candidates come from `Game._legal_target_candidates` running `TargetRequirement.rejects` over the board. `Game.choose_trigger_targets` is the scripted `DecisionScript` path used by ~34 existing scenario call sites, kept working by staging a `CHOOSE_TARGETS` answer that `put_triggers_on_stack` throws away once its batch is processed, so it cannot leak into a later, unrelated trigger of the same card name. Fixes the defect where every targeted trigger went on the stack with `targets: []` unconditionally (no production caller ever populated the old `trigger_targets: dict[str, list[TargetRef]]`), plus three bugs that dict had from being keyed by name instead of by ability instance: a stale answer reused by a later trigger, two simultaneous copies of a card unable to be targeted separately, and a delayed triggered ability inheriting another trigger's targets while ignoring `ability.controller`. | `scenarios/test_trigger_target_decisions.py` |
| An activated ability's controller is **asked** for its targets when the caller supplies none | CR 601.2c, 602.2b, 601.2e, 115.6 | `Game.activate_ability`, when handed `targets=None`, now asks through the same shared `Game._ask_targets` helper `_ask_trigger_targets` uses (`Game._ask_targets(profile, controller, source_id, source_name, text)`) rather than only ever validating whatever list a caller happened to already supply. An explicit list - including an explicit empty one - is still checked exactly as before and never re-asked. A mandatory requirement (`target_minimum` > 0) with no legal candidate makes the activation illegal outright and raises, citing CR 601.2c/602.2b - not CR 603.3d's "removed from the stack", which governs a *triggered* ability the game places unconditionally; an activation is a player's own proposed action, so CR 601.2e refuses the whole proposal instead. Fixes the reported defect: Karn, the Great Creator's +1 activated with `targets=None` went on the stack with `targets: []` and no question was ever asked (silently turning "up to one" into zero), and Mother of Runes's mandatory `{T}: Target creature you control ...` raised `ValueError: too few targets: 0 chosen ...` instead of asking who to protect. | `scenarios/test_activated_ability_target_decisions.py` |

| What a mode does on resolution | CR 700.2a, 702.42b, 700.2f, 101.3 | `Game.resolve_top` reads `Game.cast_record(spell).modes` and, when the spell was cast with modes chosen, hands off to `machines/effects.py`'s `resolve_modal_spell` instead of the ordinary card-name/oracle-text lookup - each chosen mode runs its own text independently, in the printed order (CR 702.42b generalizes past entwine: "follow the text of each of the modes in the order written on the card"), reusing the same targeted/token/search/player clause parsers a non-modal card's whole text goes through. Two shapes exist only for a mode: a mode whose grammatical subject is "target player" rather than "you" (Kozilek's Command's token-creation and scry-then-draw modes), read by `_parse_target_player_token_mode`/`_parse_target_player_mode` off the mode's own X-substituted text with its quoted granted ability intact (`substitute_x`, not the target-parsing `mode_rules_text`, which blanks quotes). **Per-mode targets are sliced, not flattened**: `Casting.choose_targets` calls `TargetingProfile.assign_to_owners` (fed by `targeting.profile_for_modes_with_owners`) to bucket one flat target announcement into one list per chosen mode instance, stored as `CastRecord.mode_targets` - CR 700.2f's "different targeting requirements for each mode" made structural. **Fail-closed per mode, not per spell**: a mode this wave cannot read is skipped and recorded in `Game.mode_notices` (a `ModeNotice`, the same "unreadable is not the same as no effect" pattern as `TargetingNotice`/`InterveningIfNotice`/`CostModifierNotice`/`LayerNotice`), while every other chosen mode of the same cast still happens - CR 101.3 excuses only "any part of an instruction that's impossible to perform", and CR 700.2/702.42b already treat each mode as its own separable instruction rather than a sub-clause of one. **X is fixed once for the whole spell** (CR 107.3a/107.3c): `Casting.x_value_at_targets` records what X was when targets were announced, and `cast()`'s `_refuse_if_x_changed_since_targets` refuses loudly if `choose_modes`'s and `determine_cost`'s independent setters of `x_value` ever disagree by the time the spell is cast, rather than letting a mode's "up to X target ..." be counted against a stale X. Kozilek's Command's mode 1 (token creation, a variable count and a quoted granted ability) and mode 3 (an X-bounded exile) both resolve correctly now, closing the reported defect. Mystic Confluence's repeatable mode runs once per time it was chosen. Not reached by this: Spree/Tiered/pawprint modal templating (`ModalProfile.understood` is already `False` for those, so `resolve_modal_spell` never runs at all), and any mode whose clause shape none of the existing parsers cover (Mystic Confluence's "unless its controller pays", Cryptic Command's "Tap all creatures your opponents control.", Thraben Charm's dynamic-damage and player's-graveyard-exile modes) - each such mode is simply a `ModeNotice`. | `scenarios/test_modal_spells.py` |

| Mana spend restrictions ("Spend this mana only to ...") | CR 106.6 (the restriction half of it; CR 703.4q / 500.5 for when the mana and the tag are lost; CR 118.3a for paying being the removal of *that* mana) | `models/mana.py` carries the tag on the mana in the pool, never on its source: `ManaRestriction` (the printed sentence plus a tuple of `SpendClause` alternatives), `RestrictedMana` (how much of `ManaPool.floating` carries each), and `ManaSpend` (what a cost is *for* - `casting(card)` against `activating(card)`, which is what tells a creature **spell** apart from an ability of a creature **source**). `ManaPool._spendable_buckets` removes mana the restriction forbids before the payment search runs, so an illegal purpose is **refused** rather than deprioritised; `ManaPool._buckets` then orders restricted mana ahead of unrestricted, so the mana that can do less is the mana spent and a legal play is never stranded. `ManaPool.empty` drops the tags with the mana. `machines/cost.py` reads the sentence (`spend_restriction`), tags what a source produces (`CostPayment.tap_for_mana`, inferring which ability was activated from what it produced when the caller does not say - `restriction_for_production`), and carries the purpose (`CostPayment.spend`, set by `Casting.determine_cost` and `Game.begin_activating`). `auto_tap` offers a restricted ability exactly when the restriction permits the cost being paid. All four printings in the pool are read: Shang-Chi, Master of Kung Fu, Eldrazi Temple, Cavern of Souls (needs CR 614.12a's chosen creature type on the permanent), Delighted Halfling. **CR 106.6's second clause is modelled too** - "an additional effect that affects the spell or ability that mana is spent on". `models/mana.ManaSpendEffect` is the tag (the printed sentence), `SpendEffectMana` says how much of the pool carries it, `_Bucket` gained a fourth slot for it beside the restriction slot, and `ManaPayment.spend_effects_spent` records which of the mana a payment actually spent carried one. Both production sites tag it (`Game.activate_ability` and `CostPayment.tap_for_mana`, off `ManaAbility.spend_effect`), `Casting._move_to_the_stack` records it on `CastRecord.spend_effects` at CR 601.2h, and `Game._apply_mana_spend_effects` turns it into the layer 6 grant (CR 613.1f) as the permanent enters, read by `machines/layers.spend_effect_effects`. Arena of Glory's haste is the printing that works end to end. An unreadable sentence gives nothing away rather than failing closed, because this clause never narrows what the mana may pay for. **Still not modelled**: the "and that spell can't be countered" form of the same clause (Cavern of Souls, Delighted Halfling) and CR 106.6's third thing, a delayed triggered ability. A payment that cannot name what it is for (`CostPayment.spend` left `None` - a foretell/plot cost, a keyword "unless you pay") gets no restricted mana at all, and a sentence the grammar cannot read produces a restriction with no clauses that permits nothing: both fail closed. Restricted mana added by an *effect* rather than a mana ability (`machines/effects.py`'s mana-adding clauses) is still added untagged. | `scenarios/test_mana_spend_restrictions.py`, `scenarios/test_mana_abilities.py`, `scenarios/test_auto_tap.py` |

| Knowledge Pool's and Possibility Storm's shared "Whenever a player casts a spell from their hand, that player exiles it[, ...]" - the exile-and-maybe-free-cast family, distinct from cascade's own "exile until a hit" shape because the exiled cast spell itself is never a candidate for its own free cast, and (Knowledge Pool only) the linked pool persists and accumulates **across** triggers rather than being consumed and bottomed by each one. Knowledge Pool: exile the cast spell, then may cast a spell from among every *other* card ever exiled with this same artifact (CR 607.1c: linked to itself), paying nothing. Possibility Storm: exile the cast spell, dig the caster's own library until a card sharing a type with it turns up, may cast that one card for free, then bottom everything this trigger exiled (the spell included) that was not cast | CR 601.2a (the third way off the stack besides resolving or being countered), CR 701.13a (exile), CR 400.7 (a new object, no targets, no cast record), CR 701.6a vs 113.6g (exiling is not countering, so "can't be countered" does not apply and no `SPELL_IS_COUNTERED` fires), CR 118.12 (the "if the player does" reads whether the mandatory exile was actually started - false once the spell is already gone, whether to a second trigger or a counter resolving first), CR 603.4 (no intervening *if*: Knowledge Pool's "If the player does" sits well past the trigger condition), CR 608.2g / 117.2e (a cast made during a resolving ability's own effect has no timing check and no priority window, so a sorcery-speed hit may be free-cast on the opponent's turn or with something else on the stack), CR 118.9/118.9a/118.9d (the free cast is CR 118.9's alternative cost), CR 607.2a/607.1c/607.3 (the linked-ability family; `Game.cards_imprinted_on` is CR 607.3's plural), CR 406.3/406.3a (face up by default - never the `Pyxis of Pandemonium` face-down shape), CR 608.2d/101.3 (an empty pool makes the free cast illegal, not merely declined), CR 302.1 (the ordinary creature-timing check CR 608.2g bypasses) | `machines/triggers.py`'s `GameEvent.origin_zone` and `_origin_zone_matches` (so the free cast, itself a cast from exile, does not re-trigger the ability that granted it - the loop this whole family would otherwise fall into the moment its own effect worked); `machines/effects.py`'s `_parse_cast_from_hand_then_exile_clause` (read against the whole, unsplit effect text - `_prepare_effect_clauses` would otherwise cut Knowledge Pool's "If the player does" away from what it is CR 118.12 anaphora for), `_knowledge_pool_free_cast_effect`, `_possibility_storm_effect`; `Game.exile_from_stack` (the exile verb, built next to but never through `Game.counter`); `Game.cards_imprinted_on`/`Game.has_legal_free_cast`/`Game.cast_free_from_exile` (parameterised `keyword`, previously hardcoded to `"Rebound"` for every caller); the shared "each player ..." exile closure in `_merge_each_player_clauses` now annotates whatever a step moves into exile with `ExileMarker.IMPRINTED` (skipped for a face-down card, so Pyxis of Pandemonium's own CR 406.3a "no characteristics" reading is untouched), which is what makes Knowledge Pool's own ETB actually populate the pool ability 2 reads. | Ordering the triggers one player controls | CR 603.3b | Whole. APNAP order between players is `machines/triggers.apnap_order`; within one player's block `Game._order_triggers_by_controller` asks that player, through `machines/decisions.DecisionKind.ORDER_TRIGGERS`/`ask_trigger_order`, and re-lays the block in the answer (bottom of the stack first). Scriptable from `Scenario.will_order_triggers` by naming each trigger's printed source. A block of one is not a choice and is not asked about; an unscripted block keeps the order it triggered in, which is what `default_answer` returns, so nothing moves unless a caller says so. | Splice | CR 702.47a, 702.47b, 702.47c, 702.47e, 612.10 | `Casting.splice(name)` in `machines/casting.py`: the card is revealed and **stays in hand**, its splice cost is folded into `additional_costs` (so CR 601.2h pays it with everything else), and its rules text is written onto `CardState.spliced_text` by `cast()` - late enough that a casting reversed under CR 601.6/CR 733.1 leaves no text behind. `CardState.oracle_text` appends it *after* the spell's own, which is the order CR 612.10 states, and `move_to` clears it on any move off the stack (CR 702.47e). "As long as this Class is level N or greater, it has [Abilities]" (cumulative, not "exactly N") is enforced at each consumer: `machines/triggers.triggers_for` skips a triggered ability whose `class_level` exceeds the current one, and `CardState.class_ability_text`/`machines.replacement.replacements_of_state` give a level-gated view of the permanent's own text to the two consumers (`Game.static_cost_modifiers_for`, the battlefield replacement sweep) that read raw oracle text rather than parsed `Ability` objects - every non-Class permanent's `class_ability_text` is exactly its ordinary `oracle_text`, so nothing else changed. Separately, `Game.resolve_top` looked up a resolving spell's effect with `effect_for(spell.card.name, spell.card.oracle_text)` - the raw `Card` fields, null for a `modal_dfc`/`transform` spell - so every modal-DFC spell (Shatterskull Smashing, Valakut Awakening, Sink into Stupor, Turntimber Symbiosis) silently resolved to nothing; `Game.cost_reduction_for`, `Game.static_cost_modifiers_for`, `Game._ability_removed` (an ability-loss check), `machines/combat.py`'s "can't be blocked" reader and evasion-notice text, and `machines/layers.py`'s `effects_for` (every layer-computed static/pump/anthem ability) had the identical bug and are fixed alongside it. `machines/targeting.targeting_profile` and `machines/modes.modal_profile` had a variant of the same defect one level down - they parse a spell's targeting/modal shape off a raw `Card`, before any `CardState` exists, so `spell.oracle_text`'s fallback was not available to them; `Card.front_oracle_text` is the new `Card`-level fallback (mirroring `Casting.base_cost`'s existing one for `mana_cost`), and both now read it instead of `card.oracle_text`. The cost shape is parsed off the ability's own text, not keyed by card name: `cost_component_for_phrase` reads the self-referential "Put N \<kind> counter(s) on this \<type>" and "Remove N \<kind> counter(s) from this \<type>" templates (Devoted Druid's -1/-1, Walking Ballista's +1/+1) into an empty-cards component that `Game.activation_cost`'s existing self-reference fill-in (the same one `exert_cost`/`exile_cost` already use) completes with the ability's own permanent. `_why_not_remove_counters` refuses the cost outright, before anything is spent, when the permanent does not have that many counters (CR 118.3, mirroring the already-tapped-permanent case the rule states); `_why_not_place_counters` only needs the permanent to still be there to receive one. Both place and remove happen at cost-payment time - during activation (CR 602.2b), before the ability that carries them ever resolves - so a -1/-1 counter's toughness drop, or a pairwise annihilation against an existing +1/+1 counter (CR 704.5q), is visible immediately, and CR 704.5f's zero-toughness state-based action can end the permanent before its own ability resolves (the classic Devoted Druid line: two activations, no Vizier of Remedies, and the second cost's counter is the one that kills it). The rewind (CR 733.1) reuses the loyalty cost's `_remember_counters`/deep-copy pattern rather than `CostComponent._remember`'s by-reference snapshot, which would otherwise record the mutation as already having happened. Not covered: a printed cost naming a *chosen* target rather than "this \<type>" (no printing in the pool needs it, and this builder has no target-resolution hook at cost-build time), and a cost that spreads a total across several permanents at once - The Filigree Sylex's "Remove ten oil counters from among permanents you control" - which is a distribution problem this builder does not attempt; both shapes fall through `cost_component_for_phrase` to `None`, and `Game.activation_cost` turns that into a raised refusal rather than a silent free activation | `scenarios/test_counters_as_a_cost.py`, `test_cost.py` |
| Characteristic-defining abilities that define P/T, in sublayer 7a | CR 604.3, 604.3a, 208.2a, 208.5, 613.4a | `define_power_toughness` (both halves) and `define_power` (power only - Nighthawk Scavenger's toughness is a plain printed number), `LayerContext`, `Characteristics.numeric_power_toughness` in `machines/layers.py`. `Game.computed_board()` builds the `LayerContext` (every graveyard, plus a per-controller mapping) fresh on every call and hands it to `ContinuousEffects.board`, so `Game.power_of`/`toughness_of`/`characteristics_of` - and everything that reads them: combat, state-based actions, targeting - see a graveyard-counting CDA's real answer rather than an empty world. Three graveyard-reading shapes are covered: every graveyard (Tarmogoyf), only the controller's own (Boneyard Wurm, via `LayerContext.graveyard_of`), and every graveyard except the controller's own (Nighthawk Scavenger, via `LayerContext.opponents_graveyard_of`) | `test_layers.py`, `scenarios/test_characteristic_defining_abilities.py` |
