| Continuous effects created by a resolving spell or ability, with a duration | CR 611.2, 611.2a, 611.2b, 611.2c, 613.7a, 613.7b, 514.2, 703.4p, 500.5b | `Duration`, `ContinuousEffects` in `machines/layers.py`; `create_continuous_effect` in `machines/effects.py`. **Fixed defect:** "Target creature gets +N/+N and gains \<keyword> until end of turn." (Give In to Violence) used to reach `machines/effects.py` and do nothing at all, with no `LayerNotice` recorded - `split_effect_clauses` cuts the sentence into a pump clause and a keyword clause on its own "and" separator before any clause parser ever saw it whole, and neither half alone matched `_CONTINUOUS_PUMP_OR_KEYWORD_RE`'s pure-pump-or-pure-keyword shape, so the card fell through `effect_for` to no effect and no record. `_merge_continuous_pump_keyword_clauses` now undoes that cut (forward order and, defensively, the unattested reverse "gains \<keyword> and gets +N/+N") generically off oracle text and hands the whole spell to `create_continuous_effect`, so it reaches `parsed_layer_effects`'s existing four-shape parser exactly like a plain "gets +N/+N until end of turn." already did - a keyword this engine does not model (Adamant Will's indestructible) or more than one granted keyword (Aerial Maneuver) still reaches that parser and comes back as a `LayerNotice` rather than silence. | `test_layers.py`, `scenarios/test_continuous_effects_with_a_duration.py`, `scenarios/test_continuous_pump_and_keyword_grant.py` |
| Putting N counters on *each member of a group* ("each creature you control", "each Cat you control"), as opposed to something targeted | CR 122.1, 205.2a, 205.3, 613.4, 613.5 | `_parse_put_counters_on_group_clause` in `machines/effects.py`, the group-wide twin of `put_counters_on_ability_targets`'s "on target X". Reads the *computed* board (CR 613.5), not the printed type line, so a permanent that gained a subtype only through a continuous effect still counts. A card-type qualifier ("creature") and a creature-subtype qualifier ("Cat") are both read off the same `[a-z]+` group - Leyline of Abundance's fixed-amount `{6}{G}{G}` activation and Ouroboroid's own "where X is this creature's power" variable-magnitude trigger. **Now proven against Ajani, Nacatl Pariah's own printed "+2: Put a +1/+1 counter on each Cat you control."**, driven through a live game: his back face's abilities are reachable since `CardState.transformed` feeds characteristics (see the "Transforming a permanent" row above), so the former xfail in `scenarios/test_mass_counter_placement.py` is a passing test that transforms him, activates the `+2` and checks the counter lands on Ocelot Pride and not on Ajani himself, who is no longer a Cat (CR 205.3). The clause shape is still also proven directly against Ocelot Pride, a real single-faced Cat creature. | `scenarios/test_mass_counter_placement.py` |
| W7a: the long tail of activated abilities - nine cards whose effect clause was previously unread | CR 122.1, 700.7, 201.5, 701.26b, 700.6, 603.12, 400.2, 702.189a, 107.3a | Nine self-contained parsers in `machines/effects.py`, none of them table rows: **Walking Ballista**'s `{4}: Put a +1/+1 counter on this creature.` and **The Filigree Sylex**'s `{T}: Put an oil counter on ...` - `_parse_put_counters_on_self_clause`, the self-referential twin of the group-wide shape above, reading `ability.source(game)` rather than a target. **Devoted Druid**'s "Put a -1/-1 counter on this creature: Untap this creature." - `_parse_untap_self_clause`; the cost side already worked (see "Counters as a cost" above), only the untap effect was missing, and the Vizier-less loop it enables terminates correctly on CR 704.5f once toughness reaches 0. Both self-referential shapes now also read a card's own **printed name** standing in for CR 700.7's "this \<type>" (CR 201.5) - `Game._self_normalized_effect_text`/`_cost_refers_to_self` - which is what The Filigree Sylex ("... on The Filigree Sylex") and Grist, the Hunger Tide ("... on Grist") both print instead; a sibling wave's own `_exiles_itself_by_name` covers the same CR 201.5 self-reference for an `Exile <name>` cost (Abstergo Entertainment's own "Exile Abstergo Entertainment"), so the two coexist rather than duplicate. The Filigree Sylex's second ability, "Destroy each nonland permanent with mana value equal to the number of oil counters on ...", reuses Engineered Explosives's existing mass-destruction-by-own-counters shape once normalised; its third ability's "Remove ten oil counters from among permanents you control" stays the distribution refusal the "Counters as a cost" row above already documents - unchanged, still correctly a raised `ValueError`, never a free activation. **The Legend of Roku // Avatar Roku**'s `{8}: Create a 4/4 red Dragon creature token with flying and firebending 4.` needed two fixes: CR 702.189a's "Firebending N" as a leveled token keyword (`_LEVELED_TOKEN_KEYWORDS`), and a whole-text token-clause parser (`_parse_whole_text_token_clause`) that reads the clause without going through `split_effect_clauses` first, whose bare-"and" cut otherwise slices "with flying and firebending 4" into two clauses neither of which parses alone - proven end to end through a real `.transform()` and `activate_ability`, now that "Transforming a permanent" (above) makes the back face's abilities reachable. **Abstergo Entertainment**'s "Return up to one target historic card from your graveyard to your hand, then exile all graveyards." needed CR 700.6's "historic" qualifier - `TargetRequirement.historic`, checked as a disjunction across card type, supertype, *and* subtype at once, a shape none of the existing per-field AND-only restrictions could express. **Agatha's Soul Cauldron**'s "Exile target card from a graveyard. When a creature card is exiled this way, put a +1/+1 counter on target creature you control." is CR 603.12's reflexive-trigger shape again, but conditioned on the *type* of what the first sentence exiled rather than on a player's choice - `_parse_exile_target_card_reflexive_creature_counter`, reusing `Game.create_reflexive_trigger` rather than a second reflexive primitive; `profile_for_text`'s reflexive-tail cut (see "Trigger conditions the matcher cannot read" below) was widened from "when you do," to also match "when \<event\> this way," so the outer ability still announces only its own target. **Grist, the Hunger Tide**'s `+1` ("... then mill a card. If an Insect card was milled this way, put a loyalty counter on Grist and repeat this process.") is a self-terminating loop reading the specific card `_mill` (now returning what it moved) just milled, not the graveyard generally - `_parse_create_token_mill_repeat_clause`. **Tyvar, Jubilant Brawler**'s `-2` ("Mill three cards, then you may return a creature card with mana value 2 or less from your graveyard to the battlefield.") is a resolution-time `MAY`/`CHOOSE_CARDS` choice among the whole graveyard after the mill (CR 400.2: no "just arrived" exception) rather than a target - `_parse_mill_then_may_return_creature_clause`. **Fiend Artisan**'s `{X}{B/G}, {T}, Sacrifice another creature: Search your library for a creature card with mana value X or less, ...` needed CR 107.3a's X extended from spells onto activated abilities for the first time: `AbilityOnStack.x_value`, threaded through `Game.activation_cost`/`begin_activating`/`activate_ability`'s new `x_value` parameter (`ManaCost.with_x` folds it into the paid cost) and read back by `activated_effect_for`'s own `substitute_x` fallback, mirroring `effect_for`'s existing spell-side handling exactly. | `scenarios/test_activated_ability_effects.py` |
| A duration-bearing team-wide pump with no target, its magnitude computed at resolution ("Creatures you control get +N/+N until end of turn", optionally "where X is the number of \<type> you control") | CR 611.2, 611.2a, 611.2c, 613.1d, 613.4c | `_MASS_PUMP_UNTIL_EOT_RE`/`_mass_pump_until_eot_builder` in `machines/layers.py` build the continuous effect (per-target `ContinuousEffect`s exactly like `_pump_and_keyword_builder`'s own single-subject shape, not a live `Selector` the way the anthem's CR 611.3 shape needs); `_mass_pump_ability_effect`/`_mass_pump_spell_effect`/`_run_mass_pump` in `machines/effects.py` compute the concrete, fixed-now group of creatures (CR 611.2c: "any objects that come under [that] control later aren't affected") and, when the card prints one, resolve the "where X is ..." magnitude through `count_expression` **once**, substituting a literal number for "X" before handing the sentence to the layers parser - never a live re-read, which is what would let a creature entering later retroactively grow the pump. Closes the row this file used to carry for "a team-wide temporary pump with no target": Violent Outburst's own "+1/+0" and Craterhoof Behemoth's "+X/+X and trample, where X is the number of creatures you control" both resolve for real now. The `Duration.UNTIL_END_OF_TURN`-only gate in `_parse_mass_pump_clause` matters: without it, this shape would misfire on Glorious Anthem's own durationless CR 611.3 anthem (which every resolving *spell* passes through `effect_for` too, permanents included) and double the bonus by storing a second, redundant copy of the same static effect. | `scenarios/test_cascade.py::test_violent_outbursts_own_pump_actually_pumps`, `scenarios/test_computed_magnitude_team_pump.py` |
| A shared count expression - "the number of \<type>\[s] you control" / "\<type> cards in your graveyard" - fixed once at the moment a caller evaluates it (CR 611.2c) | CR 205.2a, 613.4, 613.5 | `count_expression(phrase: str) -> Callable[[Game, str], int] \| None` in `machines/effects.py`, returning a function of `(game, controller)` rather than a value, so the caller decides exactly when CR 611.2c's magnitude is fixed. Reads only a single CR 300.1 card type as the qualifier ("creature[s]") - a coloured, named, or compound qualifier ("green creatures", "Human creatures", "artifact or enchantment permanents") is not read and returns `None` rather than being guessed at; no card in the pool needs one of those shapes yet. "You control" reads the computed board (CR 613.5); "in your graveyard" reads `CardState.types` directly, since a graveyard card has no continuous effects to compute. Consumed by Craterhoof Behemoth's own pump magnitude and by Grist, the Hunger Tide's -5 ("Each opponent loses life equal to the number of creature cards in your graveyard", read by `_parse_each_opponent_loses_life_equal_to`). | `scenarios/test_computed_magnitude_team_pump.py`, `scenarios/test_count_expression_life_loss.py` |
| "Target permanent becomes \<type> in addition to its other types until end of turn." - an *additive* layer 4 type change, as opposed to Karn, the Great Creator's replacing "becomes a[n] \<type> creature ..." | CR 613.1d, 611.2 | `_BECOMES_TYPE_IN_ADDITION_RE`/`_becomes_type_in_addition_builder` in `machines/layers.py`, reusing `add_type` (already load-bearing for earthbend's creature-type grant) rather than a `set`-style replacement. Liquimetal Coating is the card. | `scenarios/test_type_changing_in_addition_to.py` |
| **Board-wide land-type changes meeting each other in layer 4, and a Saga that stops being one** | CR 613.1d, 613.1f, 613.3, 613.6, 613.7, 613.7a, 613.8, 613.8a, 613.8b, 305.6, 305.7, 305.8, 205.1b, 205.4c, 611.1, 611.2, 700.4, 107.15, 303.5, 505.4, 714.2d, 714.3c, 714.4, 704.5s, 603.4, 608.2a, 106.7 | Blood Moon, Urborg, Tomb of Yawgmoth, Yavimaya, Cradle of Growth and Dryad of the Ilysian Grove are all read off their printed sentences by `machines/layers.py`'s existing shapes (`_nonbasic_lands_are_builder`, `_each_land_is_builder`, `_lands_are_every_basic_type_builder`) - no per-card rows - and the **ordering between them goes through the generic CR 613.8 dependency machinery**, which is what makes the two pairs answer differently. *Urborg/Yavimaya against Blood Moon* is a one-way dependency: applying Blood Moon strips the ability generating Urborg's effect (CR 305.7), changing its *existence* (CR 613.8a clause b), so Blood Moon applies first **whatever the timestamps** and nothing is ever a Swamp. *Dryad against Blood Moon* has no dependency in either direction - "nonbasic" is a supertype question (CR 205.4c, 305.8) - so CR 613.7's timestamps decide and **the two orders give different boards**. Reaching that second answer needed a fix to `machines/layers._writes_differently`: it compared the absolute characteristics an effect landed on, so an additive effect meeting a setting effect looked dependent purely because of the setting effect's own handiwork, and the pair collapsed into one order. It now compares, for a collection-valued field, the elements the effect adds and removes, discounting whatever `other` changed on that object; scalar fields still compare outcomes, which is what keeps Humility and Opalescence independent in 7b. **A granted basic land type is now usable, not just visible** (this closes the old "a land type *added* by an effect is invisible to activation" gap): `machines/triggers.added_ability_lines` is the additive twin of `rewritten_ability_lines`, and `Game.activated_abilities`, `Game.colors_could_produce`, `machines/cost._activations_of` and `CostPayment.tap_for_mana` all append it, so a Forest under an Urborg really taps for `{B}` and a land under a Dryad is a five-colour source. Appended last, so every printed ability keeps its index. **Urza's Saga under Blood Moon** is the corner case the Saga half exists for. `CardState.is_saga` and `.chapter_numbers` are now layer-aware (CR 613.5) - the same shape `controller`/`power`/`toughness` already had - so CR 305.7 taking the Saga subtype and the three chapter abilities away means CR 505.4/714.3c stop giving it lore counters and CR 714.4/704.5s never apply, because both are gated on "a Saga permanent **with one or more chapter abilities**". It is therefore **never sacrificed**, and CR 714.2d's "final chapter number is 0" trap is disarmed by that same qualifier rather than by the count. The abilities chapters I and II already granted are separate continuous effects (CR 611.2) applied in layer 6, after layer 4, so they survive: the land taps for `{C}` **or** `{R}` and still makes Constructs. Three call sites had been dropping grants whenever the layers rewrote the printed lines (`Game.activated_abilities`, `Game._still_has_ability`, `machines/cost._activations_of`) and now append them. **Valakut, the Molten Pinnacle** needed the trigger side: `Game._enters_event` now reads `Game.characteristics_of` (CR 613.5) so a Forest entering under a Dryad is a *Mountain* entering; `machines/triggers._ENTERING_SUBTYPE`/`_required_entering_subtype` make an entry condition honour a named subtype at all (it previously checked card types only, so any land fired a Mountain trigger); and `_controlled_subtype_count_holds` reads "you control at least five other Mountains" as a live, layer-aware, CR 700.4-respecting board count, evaluated both on trigger (CR 603.4) and again on resolution (CR 608.2a). **Not modelled**: `knowledge_search` truncates **CR 305.7** at "and any" on every phrasing tried, so the rule's tail is unverified and nothing here relies on it. Valakut's own trigger is switched off by Blood Moon (CR 305.7 removes it), which is correct and worth knowing. | `scenarios/test_land_type_changing_layers.py`, `scenarios/test_urzas_saga_under_blood_moon.py`, `scenarios/test_valakut_mountain_triggers.py` |

| Cascade | CR 702.85a, 702.85c | `_cascade_ability` and `cascade` in `machines/keywords.py`; the source is the *spell*, which `Game.emit` asks because CR 702.85a functions on the stack. The "you may cast it without paying its mana cost" half is asked for real - `DecisionKind.MAY` (CR 608.2d), gated on `why_cannot_cast_restriction` (CR 611.1's flat prohibitions, not CR 117.1a's timing, which is a question for whenever the caller later drives `Casting.use_permission()`) - and on "yes" hands out a `PlayPermission` (CR 601.3) annotated `ExileMarker.CASCADE`. Declining (or the hit being flatly uncastable) puts it on the bottom in the same random shuffle the misses get, exactly as CR 702.85a's "put the exiled cards on the bottom" reads when nothing left exile some other way. **A second, related defect is now closed too**: unlike `machines/casting.finish_suspend`'s permission (legitimately left to expire unused, since CR 702.62a's own sentence names "if you don't, it remains exiled" as the fallback) or Ugin/Ragavan's explicit "until end of turn" grants, cascade's own single sentence names no fallback other than the bottom for *every* card that "weren't cast" - an accepted-but-never-driven cast is not a legal reason for the hit to stay exiled. `PlayPermission.bottom_if_unused` marks cascade's grant, and `Game._bottom_unused_cascade_permissions` - called from `cleanup_step` right before CR 514.2 would otherwise just drop the expiring permission - sweeps a still-unclaimed hit to the bottom instead. Found live: a real game log (09d1f97f605c4eada0021ed441306e81, seq 150-157) showed the hit exiled and then simply abandoned there - never offered the choice, never bottomed - because the old `cascade` bottomed every exiled card *except* the hit, unconditionally, with no permission ever granted; the follow-up fix that first offered the choice left the second half of the same failure mode open (a granted-but-never-used permission expiring with the card still in exile), which this closes. **Not modelled**: the CR's own additional condition on the free cast, "if the resulting spell's mana value is less than this spell's mana value" - no printing in this pool casts the hit under a cost-altering effect that would change its mana value between exile and cast, so this is recorded rather than guessed at | `scenarios/test_cascade.py::test_accepting_the_cascade_permission_casts_the_hit_through_the_real_engine`, `scenarios/test_cascade.py::test_declining_the_cascade_permission_bottoms_the_hit_and_empties_exile`, `scenarios/test_cascade.py::test_accepting_the_permission_but_never_using_it_still_bottoms_the_hit` |

| **Fixed defect, real-game report:** a spell whose `Game.ask` refuses a mid-resolution answer used to vanish from every zone | CR 400.1, 400.11, 307.2, 304.2, 608.2n, 733.1 | `Game.resolve_top` pops the resolving spell off `self.stack` before calling its `machines/effects.py` effect, so a `ValueError` `Game.ask` raises for an illegal or unanswerable CR 608.2d answer - Malevolent Rumble's own `choose_cards` with `card_ids: []` was the reported case, game 535264e8458e461d874d0d38f4b1866e, seq 115-122 - used to unwind out of `resolve_top` with the spell already off the stack and nowhere else, so the game's decklist came up 59 of 60 cards. `resolve_top` now catches any exception `effect(self, spell)` raises, sends the spell to its owner's graveyard the way CR 307.2/304.2/608.2n already mandate as the final step of every instant's or sorcery's resolution (and CR 608.3e already does for a permanent spell that can't enter), the same CR 733.1 "reversed, no half-applied effect" posture `machines/cost.py`'s own `unpay` already follows for an aborted payment, and re-raises so the caller still learns the refusal at the moment it was given. `Game.decisions.asked` already recorded the refused question before the raise, so nothing about the refusal itself was ever lost - only the spell was. `Scenario.assert_in_exactly_one_zone`/`Scenario.all_card_states` (`tests/scenarios/harness.py`) are the general CR 400.1 invariant helpers this defect made worth having | `scenarios/test_decisions_during_resolution.py::test_an_empty_choose_cards_answer_is_refused_without_losing_the_spell` |
| **Fixed defect, follow-up sweep:** an *ability's* own `Game.ask` refusing a mid-resolution answer used to skip CR 608.2n's own removal-from-stack bookkeeping and CR 704.3's state-based-action check | CR 113.3, 608.2c, 608.2n, 603.3, 704.3, 733.1 | The row above fixed a resolving *spell's* own vanishing act; `Game._resolve_ability` had no equivalent guard at all, since an ability is not a card (CR 405.1) and was never at risk of vanishing from a zone the same way. A full sweep of every `game.ask(`/`game.may(` call site in `machines/effects.py` found four abilities whose paragraph (CR 113.3) mutates unconditionally - Fight Rigging's `+1/+1` counter, Cori-Steel Cutter's Flurry token, Tyvar's and Six's own mill - before a later, optional clause of the *same* paragraph asks its own CR 608.2d question. CR 733.1's reversal has nothing to undo there: the first clause already completed, in full, exactly as CR 608.2c's printed order requires, so none of those four mutations are rolled back. What was missing is `Game._resolve_ability`'s own bookkeeping continuing to run when the later question can't be legally answered: `self.delayed_triggers.discard(ability)` (CR 608.2n's "ceases to exist") and `self._before_priority()` (CR 704.3's state-based-action check, then CR 603.3's `put_triggers_on_stack`) now run from an `except` clause that re-raises afterwards, mirroring `Game.resolve_top`'s own catch above. Made observable with Impact Tremors: its own "whenever a creature you control enters" trigger is recorded straight into `Game.pending_triggers` the instant Cori-Steel Cutter's token enters, independently of whether Flurry's own resolution later raises - without the fix that trigger stayed stuck in `pending_triggers`, never reaching the stack at all, rather than merely resolving late | `scenarios/test_fight_rigging.py::test_an_illegal_free_cast_answer_still_lands_the_counter_and_completes_resolution`, `scenarios/test_flurry_ordinal_trigger.py::test_an_illegal_attach_answer_still_leaves_the_monk_created_and_completes_resolution`, `scenarios/test_activated_ability_effects.py::test_an_illegal_choose_answer_still_leaves_the_mill_and_completes_resolution`, `scenarios/test_six.py::test_an_illegal_choose_answer_still_leaves_the_mill_and_completes_resolution` |

It was a per-candidate `MAY` walk until a live seat activated Wooded Foothills for a Breeding Pool, declined once, and was handed a basic Forest - a searcher could only decline what it was shown, never name what it wanted, and after CR 701.24b's shuffle the order it is shown in is arbitrary. `optional=True` is CR 701.23b (declinable, `exact=False`, finding nothing is a real answer); `optional=False` is CR 701.23d (as many as possible, `exact=True`, the choice is *which* not *whether*), and a mandatory search with no more candidates than it takes is not asked at all. The default for an unscripted seat is `Decision.default_cards`: find, in zone order | `scenarios/test_searching_a_zone.py`, `scenarios/test_shuffling.py`, `scenarios/test_fetching_a_land.py` |

| The layer engine reaching the state-based actions | CR 613.5, 704.5f, 704.5g | `Game.computed_board`/`power_of`/`toughness_of`, `_dies_to_state_based_action`. **`CardState` itself can ask now**: `CardState.bind_to_game(game)` makes `.power`/`.toughness` answer with `Game.characteristics_of` for a permanent on the battlefield and fall back to the printed value plus counters (CR 122.1a) everywhere else, which is what gives a Tarmogoyf's printed `*` a number at all (CR 604.3, CR 613.4a) and what an anthem needs to reach `machines/combat.py`'s CR 510.1a. **It is called now, on both routes onto a battlefield**: `Game.enter_the_battlefield` binds a permanent as it enters, which covers every route in a real game (a permanent spell resolving, a land being played, an effect putting one there), and `tests/scenarios/harness.py`'s `Scenario.build` binds a staged board the same way, so a scenario board is not a place where a permanent reports printed characteristics while a played one reports computed ones. That closed the three xfails that turned on it (`test_characteristic_defining_abilities.py::test_the_game_agrees_with_the_layer_engine_about_a_goyfs_power`, `test_static_abilities.py::test_the_game_sees_the_anthem_a_static_ability_generates`, `judge/test_judge_state_based_actions.py::test_tarmogoyf_lives_or_dies_by_its_toughness_right_now`) and made two assertions elsewhere visibly stale rather than merely lucky: `judge/test_judge_layers.py`'s Twisted Image test pinned "the switch never reaches `CardState`" and now reads the 7d switch (CR 613.4d) back off the permanent, and `test_comparison_targeting.py` used `CardState.power` as a stand-in for the printed power, which stops being a stand-in the moment the object is bound - it reads `CardState.card.power` now. A `CardState` is unbound only when built outside a game or rebuilt by `model_validate`, and that fallback is still covered | `scenarios/judge/test_judge_state_based_actions.py`, `scenarios/test_card_state_computed_characteristics.py` |

| Static cost increases printed on *other* permanents, and Trinisphere's floor | CR 601.2f, 611.3 | `Game.static_cost_modifiers_for` (increases before reductions in `apply_modifications`, the floor applied last), parsed off oracle text by `cost_modifier_effect`/`looks_like_unparsed_cost_modifier` in `machines/cost.py` - unconditional ("Spells cost {N} more to cast"), type-qualified ("Noncreature"/"Nonartifact" spells), per-prior-spell-this-turn (Damping Sphere), the Trinisphere floor shape, and the name-scoped shape (`CostModifierKind.NAMED_INCREASE`, Disruptor Flute's "Spells with the chosen name cost {3} more to cast" - the amount alone cannot say who it taxes, so `static_cost_modifiers_for` compares CR 614.12a's recorded choice on `CardState.choice` against the spell's name, and a source with no recorded choice taxes nothing and files a notice). | Protection, the targeting half | CR 702.16a, 702.16b, 702.16g, 601.2c | `protection_qualities`, `Game.protection_refusal`, consulted by `Game.legal_target` on resolution (CR 608.2b) **and now by `TargetingProfile.check` at announcement (CR 601.2c)**: `TargetingProfile.source_card` carries the card a spell's profile was read off, so `machines/targeting.py` can ask the game the same protection question rather than growing a second, divergent copy of it - a protected target is refused as the spell is cast instead of being announced and then fizzling. A profile built from bare text (`profile_for_text`, an ability's own words) has no `source_card` and is still left to the resolution recheck, as is a spell whose profile comes from X-substituted or split-half text. `Game._effective_protection_qualities` folds in a *granted* protection quality too (see the row above), so this reads a permanent's own printing plus whatever another static ability has given it | `scenarios/test_protection.py`, `scenarios/test_protection_grants.py` |
| A plain "can't be blocked" restriction | CR 509.1a, 613.5 | `combat.evasion_restriction_refusal`, read off the attacker's own *abilities* rather than off `Keyword` - unlike flying, menace, or landwalk, this restriction has no keyword name of its own. The sentences come from `Game.characteristics_of` when there is a game (CR 613.5), so Dress Down's layer 6 "Creatures lose all abilities" takes the restriction away and the block becomes legal; the printed text is read only when there is no board to compute. Two shapes: unconditional (The Mindskinner) and "can't be blocked by creatures with power N or less" (Questing Beast). A subject is only read self-referentially ("this creature" or the card's own short name before a comma, CR 201.4-style); nothing in the pool grants "can't be blocked" to *another* creature, so that shape is not read. A sentence that opens the same way but is not one of the two - "power N or **greater**" (April O'Neil, Kunoichi Trainee), a colour, or any other qualifier - is not guessed at: `combat.looks_like_unparsed_evasion_restriction` catches it and `block_refusal` records an `EvasionNotice` (dedup'd per attacker) instead of the block silently going through unrestricted *or* silently being refused | `scenarios/test_evasion_restrictions.py` |

| Mass destruction and mass sacrifice: "destroy each `<qualifier>`" / "each player sacrifices all `<qualifier>`" | CR 701.8a, 701.21a, 608.2f, 202.3, 712.8f | `_parse_mass_type_qualifier` reads the qualifier (an optional "nonland " prefix, "permanent[s]" with no restriction, "colored"/"colorless", or a comma/"and"/"or"-joined list of card types with no trailing "permanent" at all - Wrath of the Skies's own template) off the *computed* board (`Game.characteristics_of`); `_parse_mass_mv_rhs`/`_parse_mass_mv_comparison` read the numeric comparison's right-hand side as either a printed constant (Culling Ritual) or a count of counters on the clause's own source (Engineered Explosives's "the number of charge counters on this artifact", via `last_known_counters` once the source has already left as its own activation's cost); `_MASS_WITHOUT_COUNTER_RE` reads an absence-of-a-counter qualifier (Oblivion Stone). `_destroy_matching` snapshots the battlefield once and rechecks each candidate's zone immediately before its own `destroy` call, the same CR 608.2b-shaped recheck `_parse_x_energy_pay_destroy_by_mana_value` already uses for Wrath of the Skies, which shares this grammar but keeps its own dedicated parser above it for its energy-payment right-hand side. `each_player_sacrifices_colored_permanents` is All Is Dust's own dedicated whole-sentence parser (sacrifice, not destroy, so CR 702.12b never applies) rather than reusing the qualifier grammar, since its "permanents ... that are one or more colors" is a relative clause, not an adjective-noun qualifier. `machines/layers.printed_characteristics`'s own `mana_value` also now follows `state.land_face_played` (CR 712.8f) instead of always reading `Card.cmc`, which is Scryfall's *spell*-face mana value for a modal double-faced card even when its land face is up | `scenarios/test_mass_destruction.py` |

| A counterspell's own qualifier, and CR 118.12a's "unless" rider | CR 701.5a, 109.2b, 118.12a, 608.2b | `_COUNTER_TARGET_SPELL_RE` in `machines/effects.py` now accepts an optional qualifier ("noncreature", a colour, a card type) ahead of "spell" - the verb still runs `counter_targets` unconditionally, since CR 115's own qualifier legality is `machines/targeting.py`'s job, not this dispatcher's. "Counter target spell unless its controller pays {N}." (Censor, Mystical Dispute) is `_counter_unless_pays_effect`/`Game.may_pay_mana`, a new `DecisionKind.PAY_MANA` read as CR 118.12a's "may/if not" pair and put to the *targeted* spell's controller, not the counterspell's caster. Only mana already floating in that player's pool can pay it - CR 608.2g's "activate a mana ability right here" is not modelled, so a controller who would need to tap a land to pay is refused as if they had no mana at all **Widened past a single type/colour word:** the qualifier slot now takes a printed disjunction (Flusterstorm's "instant or sorcery spell"), the noun may be followed by a **predicate** on the spell's own characteristics (Spell Snare's "with mana value 2", CR 202.3e; Stern Scolding's "with power or toughness 2 or less"), and the same qualifier and predicate slots sit on the "unless its controller pays {N}" shape as well, which is what reaches Spell Pierce and Flusterstorm. Every one of those restrictions is still enforced in `machines/targeting.py` (CR 601.2c at announcement, CR 608.2b on resolution) and read-and-discarded here. Stubborn Denial's ferocious sentence (CR 207.2c: an ability word has no rules meaning) is paired onto its own "unless" clause by `_parse_counter_unless_pays_then_condition_upgrade_pair`, because "counter that spell **instead**" replaces that clause's outcome rather than running after it - with a creature of power 4 or greater (read off `Game.power_of`, CR 613.5) nobody is offered the payment at all. Disrupting Shoal's "Counter target spell if its mana value is X." is read by `_parse_counter_if_mana_value_clause` once X is known; its own CR 118.9 pitch cost is still unreadable by `machines/cost.py`, so no X is ever recorded in a real game and the card still fails closed there. | `scenarios/test_counterspells.py` |
| A counterspell's own redirect and rider, read as one paired clause rather than two independent ones | CR 400.7, 608.2c | Force of Negation's "Counter target noncreature spell. If that spell is countered this way, exile it instead of putting it into its owner's graveyard." is `_parse_counter_then_exile_instead_of_graveyard_pair`, and `Game.counter` grew a `to_exile` flag for the redirect itself. Access Denied's "Counter target spell. Create X 1/1 ... tokens ..., where X is that spell's mana value." is `_parse_counter_then_create_tokens_by_mana_value_pair`, reading the countered spell's `card.cmc` before it leaves the stack. Both fail the *whole* pair closed (no counter at all) if either sentence does not parse, the same all-or-nothing rule `_parse_anaphoric_clause_pair` already keeps | `scenarios/test_counterspells.py` |
| The CR 118.9 pitch alternative cost, read off inline text rather than a keyword | CR 118.9, 118.9c | `machines/cost.alternative_cost_offers` reads "You may `<cost>` rather than pay this spell's mana cost." (Force of Will) and its "If it's not your turn, you may `<cost>` ..." cousin (Force of Negation, Force of Vigor) off the card's own text; `Casting.use_pitch_cost` builds the `TotalCost` through the existing `total_cost_for_phrase`/`exile_cost`/`CardRequirement` machinery `_option_from_keyword` already used for Evoke and Dash, and refuses the "not your turn" gate when it is the caster's own turn. `_split_cost_phrase` now also splits on a bare "and" (no comma), which Force of Will's "pay 1 life and exile a blue card from your hand" needs and no existing keyword cost had | `scenarios/test_counterspells.py` |
| **Finding** an alternative cost, and announcing the one that was picked | CR 118.9, 118.9a, 118.9b, 118.9c, 118.9d, 601.2b, 604.2, 604.6, 205.3i | Found live: a real game had a seat wanting to pay Force of Negation's pitch cost and no way to reach it - `use_keyword`, `use_permission`, and `use_pitch_cost` each announced *one* origin of a CR 118.9 alternative cost and none of them could be discovered without already knowing which one a given card used. `Casting.alternative_costs` is the single listing across all four origins (the spell's own CR 604.6 printed offer, a keyword whose `pays_mana_cost` is false, a `PlayPermission` granted "without paying its mana cost", and another permanent's CR 604.2 static ability), each entry carrying `index`, `source` (`AlternativeCostSource`), `rule`, `description`, `requires`, and `unavailable`; an option that exists but cannot be used *right now* is listed with its reason rather than hidden, because CR 118.9b makes an alternative cost a choice and "no such option" is a different answer from "an option you may decline". `Casting.use_alternative_cost(which=None, *, choosing=[...])` announces one by `index`, by keyword/source name, or - when exactly one is available - by nothing at all, refusing a second one under CR 118.9a and delegating to `use_keyword`/`use_permission` for those origins so there is one announcement path, not four. `use_pitch_cost` is kept as the printed-offer-only front door onto the same code. Omniscience is the CR 604.2 half: `cost.static_free_cast_grant` reads "You may cast spells from your hand without paying their mana costs." off a genuine `AbilityKind.STATIC` line of a permanent the caster controls, scoped to the zone (and card type, if the sentence names one) it prints, and `Casting.free_cast_source` records which permanent applied it. One real bug fell out of covering Fireblast: `CardRequirement.why_not` matched a cost's subtype word literally, so "Sacrifice two **Mountains**" refused every Mountain on the battlefield - the plural is now accepted alongside the singular rather than stripped, since CR 205.3i's own land-type list contains `Plains` and `Locus` | `scenarios/test_alternative_costs.py` |
| A compound target across two different noun *kinds*, now read rather than left unchecked | CR 115.2b, 105.2c | Consign to Memory's "Counter target triggered ability or colorless spell." used to be *actively misread*: `targeting.py`'s `_ABILITY_PHRASE.match` (a bare prefix match) claimed the whole compound was "target activated or triggered ability" and silently dropped "or colorless spell" - `understood=True` with a wrong, enforced restriction, which is worse than not checking at all. A later fix made the ability phrase consume the entire noun phrase before matching, which correctly fell through to `understood=False` (unchecked) instead - safe, but still not a real read of the card. `_ABILITY_OR_COLOR_COUNT_SPELL_RE` now reads the genuine disjunction: one `TargetRequirement` carries `allows_abilities=True` *and* `allows_cards=True`/`color_count="colorless"` together, since `TargetRequirement.rejects` already dispatches an ability target to `_rejects_ability` and a card target to `_rejects_card` before either field is consulted, so the two halves never interfere with each other on the same requirement. A colorless spell and a triggered ability are both legal targets now; a colored spell is correctly refused at announcement (CR 601.2c) | `scenarios/test_counterspells.py` |
| An activated ability that functions from a hand, a graveyard, or exile, printed like any other `[Cost]: [Effect.]` line rather than a CR 702 keyword | CR 113.6j, 113.6m, 702.62b, 602.5b, 113.7a | `abilities.py` reads a `zone`/`zone_rule` off any activated ability whose own cost discards itself (Boseiju's and Otawara's Channel, Waker of Waves' plain ability - CR 113.6j), whose effect returns itself from its own graveyard (World Breaker - CR 113.6m), or whose own "Activate only if this card is suspended" restriction can only ever be true in exile (Greater Gargadon's "Sacrifice an artifact, creature, or land: Remove a time counter from this card." - CR 702.62b, since "suspended" itself means "in the exile zone" among other things), off the words alone, no card names. `KeywordActivation.why_not` gates the exile case on that same `"CR 702.62b"` tag, checking the new `CardState.is_suspended` predicate (exiled, carries the suspend marker, and still has a time counter - the zone alone is not enough, since a card exiled with its last counter already gone is no longer "suspended") - and, because exile is one shared pile rather than a per-player list the way a hand or graveyard is (`Game.shared_zones`), the ownership half of the same check reads `CardState.owner` instead of a per-player `cards_in` lookup, which would otherwise always come back empty for a shared zone. Greater Gargadon's own disjunctive cost - "Sacrifice an artifact, creature, or land" - needed two more general fixes to reach `machines.cost.CardRequirement.types` (already a same-field disjunction by its own docstring): `abilities._merge_disjunctive_sacrifice_cost` re-joins the comma-and-"or" list's own top-level commas, which `_split_top_level` otherwise read as three separate costs; and `cost._requirement` strips each word's own list punctuation and skips the bare connectors ("or", "and") rather than failing the whole phrase. The effect itself - "Remove a time counter from this card." - is `effects._parse_remove_time_counter_from_self_clause`, which calls `casting.remove_suspend_time_counter` and then `casting.finish_suspend` in the same breath: removing the *last* counter this way reaches the identical `PlayPermission` grant the upkeep route already hands out, proved end to end (sacrifice down to the last counter, then actually cast the card for free) in `scenarios/test_suspend_sacrifice_activation.py`. `machines/keywords.off_battlefield_activation` is the general form of `KeywordActivation` cycling and equip already used, built from the printed ability rather than a keyword's fixed expansion; its `generic_effect=True` leaves the ability's resolution to the ordinary per-card path (`Game._effect_of`'s `ActivatedAbility` branch) rather than a `KEYWORD_EFFECTS` row, so the same clause parsers a battlefield ability reaches apply here too. A trailing "This ability costs `[N]` less to activate for each `[quality]` you control" sentence (Boseiju, Otawara) is a **separate static ability** sharing the printed line, not a third clause of the one before it (CR 604.1, and CR 702.41a's Affinity / CR 702.125a's Undaunted are the CR's own precedent for this exact shape) - `_split_off_trailing_cost_reduction` peels it off before `ActivatedAbility.effect` is built, and `Game.activation_cost_reduction_for` reads the quality generically (a CR 205.4a supertype and/or CR 205.2a card type) and applies it through the ordinary `ManaCost.minus` (CR 118.7). World Breaker's own self-return ("Return this card from your graveyard to your hand") is `_parse_self_return_clause`, a plain zone-change of the ability's own source rather than a target. Boseiju's search clause ("search their library for a land card with a basic land type") needed one more fix: `_has_a_basic_land_type` reads CR 305.6's *subtype* question, distinct from `_quality_matcher`'s "basic land" branch which checks the "basic" *supertype* (CR 305.8) - a shockland has a basic land type without being a basic land, so folding the two together would have been narrower than the card actually reads. Waker of Waves' split needed `Game.look_at_top_and_split` (a different wave's primitive); `_merge_look_top_split_hand_graveyard_clauses` is its own consumer of that primitive, alongside `_merge_look_top_reveal_to_hand_clauses`'s Duskwatch Recruiter | `scenarios/test_off_battlefield_activation.py`, `scenarios/test_suspend_sacrifice_activation.py` |
| Chalice of the Void's "mana value equal to charge counters" trigger, and "counter that spell" read generically | CR 202.3e, 107.3g, 107.3i, 607.1, 701.6a | `condition_matches`'s `_MANA_VALUE_EQUALS_COUNTERS` shape already recognised the cast-trigger condition, but compared the charge-counter count against the spell's printed `Card.cmc` - always the off-stack, X-as-0 reading (CR 107.3g), never the real on-stack value CR 202.3e asks for. `Game.put_on_stack` now computes that value itself - the printed cmc plus `x_value` once per `{X}` in the mana cost (CR 107.3i: every `{X}` on one object shares the one chosen value, so Walking Ballista's `{X}{X}` cast for X=1 is mana value 2, not 1) - and carries it on `GameEvent.amount`, which the condition reads in preference to `subject_card.cmc`. The effect side had no entry at all, and is not a `TRIGGER_EFFECTS` row: `_parse_counter_that_spell_clause` in `machines/effects.py` reads "counter that spell" by shape, through `trigger_effect_for`'s ordinary `parse_mixed_effect` fallback - CR 607.1 linked-ability anaphora back to the trigger's own event subject, not a target, so it reads `ability.event.subject_id` and counters with `Game.counter` (CR 701.6a) regardless of who cast it, Chalice's own controller included. Vexing Bauble's identical clause needed the exact same read, so its old hand-written `vexing_bauble_counter`/`TRIGGER_EFFECTS` row is deleted too - `test_mana_spent_intervening_if.py`'s Vexing Bauble test now runs entirely off this parser | `scenarios/test_chalice_of_the_void.py`, `scenarios/test_mana_spent_intervening_if.py` |
| A "Whenever you cast a `<restriction>` spell" condition's color, colorless, and mana-value restrictions | CR 105.2a, 105.2b, 105.2c, 202.2b, 202.3 | **Fixed defect, real-game report:** `condition_matches`'s `CAST_SPELL` branch checked only `_types_match` (card-type words) against the condition, so Sanctum of Ugin's "a colorless spell with mana value 7 or greater" - no type word at all - matched *any* spell cast, not only a colorless one of mana value 7 or greater; the trigger fired for Malevolent Rumble, a green mana value 2 sorcery. `GameEvent.subject_colors` now carries the cast spell's colors (`CardState.colors`, up-face aware, mirroring `subject_types`'s existing fix), and `condition_matches` reads three more clauses of the same condition: `_color_matches`/`_color_condition` for CR 105.2's colorless/monocolored/multicolored/named-color vocabulary, `_mana_value_comparison` for CR 202.3's "N or greater"/"N or less"/"greater than N"/"less than N"/bare-N comparisons (tried in that order so a bare `\d+` never eats the leading digit of "7 or greater"), and `_required_cast_subtype`/`_subtype_matches` for a capitalized subtype word immediately before "spell" (Alania, Divergent Storm's "Otter spell" shape, disambiguated from the lowercase type/color words by templating's own capitalization convention). A mana-value clause the condition names but none of the five comparison regexes matches fails closed and is recorded on `game.cast_condition_notices` (`CastConditionNotice`) rather than silently passing - the same "recognised but unreadable is not the same as absent" rule `intervening_if_holds` already applies. Ugin, Eye of the Storms' "Whenever you cast a colorless spell" trigger had the identical gap and is fixed the same way; its *effect's* own "permanent that's one or more colors" target restriction was a separate gap and is now fixed too (see its own row below) | `scenarios/test_cast_triggers.py::test_sanctums_trigger_ignores_a_cheap_colored_spell_but_fires_off_a_big_colorless_one` |
| **A printed additional cost, actually charged** | CR 118.8a, 118.8b, 118.8d, 601.2b, 601.2f, 601.2h, 118.3 | `Casting._charge_printed_additional_costs` reads the card's own "As an additional cost to cast this spell, ..." sentences at CR 601.2f and folds each into `additional_costs`. Before this, `machines/cost.additional_cost_phrases` existed and was called from **nowhere** in `src/` - every printed additional cost in the pool was free, so Fling cost `{1}{R}`, sacrificed nothing and dealt 0 damage. The objects are announced at CR 601.2b through `Casting.sacrificing`/`discarding`/`exiling`/`tapping` (the vocabulary `Game.activation_cost` already used), threaded by `Scenario.cast`/`cast_onto_stack`/`cast_and_resolve`. CR 107.3a's X is substituted first, so Toxic Deluge's "pay X life" is charged as the announced X. CR 118.8b's optional costs (a sentence opening "you may ..." - March of Otherworldly Light, Requiting Hex) are skipped, since declining is a legal announcement. A **mandatory** sentence the parser cannot read raises rather than being charged nothing - Redirect Lightning's disjunctive "pay 5 life or pay {2}" is the one such printing in the pool and has no choice mechanism yet | `scenarios/test_costs.py`, `scenarios/test_additional_cost_referenced_at_resolution.py`, `scenarios/test_toxic_deluge.py` |
| Mill as a **cost** | CR 701.17a, 701.17b, 601.2h, 118.3 | `machines/cost.mill_cost` and `_PHRASE_MILL` read Millikin's `{T}, Mill a card:`. CR 701.17b's "if given the choice to do so, they can't choose to take that action" is why a library shorter than the count refuses the payment outright instead of milling as many as possible - that second half is for an *effect* that mills, never for a cost. `CostPayment.pay` holds mill components back to CR 601.2h's second pass ("then they pay all remaining costs"), since milling moves objects from a library to a public zone | `scenarios/test_activated_abilities.py` |
| The order the parts of one total cost are paid in | CR 601.2h | `CostPayment.components_before_mana`. CR 601.2h's "in any order" is observable - Momentous Fall sacrificing Omnath, Locus of Mana reads a 5/5 if the sacrifice is paid while the green mana is still floating and a 1/1 if the mana goes first (DDR#955). Defaults to False, the mana-first order the engine always had. Momentous Fall's own resolution is read now too (`machines/effects._parse_draw_and_gain_off_sacrificed_clause`, off `Game.cast_record(spell).additional_cost_cards`), so the payout is observable - but only off the sacrificed creature's printed box plus its counters. **CR 608.2h's last known information is answered now**: `CardState.last_known_power`/`.last_known_toughness` are stamped by `Game._note_left_battlefield` while the permanent is still on the battlefield (the last instant CR 613.5 answers for it), and record only what a *continuous effect* was contributing - the printing survives a zone change and CR 122.2 deliberately destroys the counters, so neither needs a snapshot. `machines/layers.LAYER_EFFECTS` has an "Omnath, Locus of Mana" row reading the controller's unspent green off `LayerContext.unspent_mana_by_controller`, filled by `Game.computed_board` from `Game.unspent_mana_of` - which consults the open `CostPayment`'s working pool, because CR 601.2g's mana goes there and the player's own `ManaPool` does not see it until the payment writes the remainder back | `judge/test_judge_cost_payment_order.py` |

The plural, counted twin - "Create X tokens that are copies of target creature you control, except they have '\<ability>'" (Aggressive Biomancy) - is `_parse_create_token_copies_except_clause`, matched against the *whole* unsplit text because the quoted ability ends in a full stop `split_effect_clauses` would cut at, and reached on `effect_for`'s second pass once CR 107.3a's announced X has been substituted in | `scenarios/test_token_effect_parser.py` |

Wear // Tear's own existing test was passing for the wrong reason (Rancor, its only test target, was never legally on the battlefield to begin with - CR 704.5m put it in the graveyard before Tear was ever cast - so the assertion held whether or not Tear's effect ran); fixed by giving Rancor a creature to attach to and asserting `not Game.spell_fizzles(spell)` directly. `_resolution_profile` now reads `spell.oracle_text` (already face-aware via `CardState.up_face`) when `spell.card.layout is Layout.SPLIT` and `spell.face_played` names a half | `scenarios/test_split_cards.py`, `scenarios/test_token_effect_parser.py` |
| An activated ability's effect reaching the layer engine with its own resolved targets, not just its source, including CR 115.6's zero-targets-chosen case | CR 611.2, 602.2, 606, 115.6, 601.2c | **Fixed defect, reported from real play (game 433dff95eae24b04a780a960bd2445b7, seq 240-242).** `machines/effects.py`'s `create_continuous_effect_of_ability` used to branch on `if ability.targets else [source]` - but `ability.targets` being an empty list is exactly what CR 115.6's "up to one target ..., zero chosen" case looks like *and* exactly what an ability with no target clause at all (Basking Rootwalla's pump) looks like, so the two were indistinguishable and the zero-target case silently fell through to `[source]`. Reported case: Karn, the Great Creator's +1 activated with zero targets chosen animated **Karn himself** into a 4/4 - wrong twice over, since Karn (Legendary Planeswalker) was never a legal "target noncreature artifact" to begin with (CR 601.2c), and CR 115.6 requires a legally-chosen zero to affect nothing, never redirect onto the ability's own source. Fixed by reading whether the ability's *own printed text* requires a target at all - `profile_for_text(ability.ability.text)`, the same call `Game.activate_ability` already makes to check announced targets' legality at CR 601.2c/602.2b - rather than inferring it from how many targets came back. When the text requires a target, `game.resolve_card_targets(ability)` is used (the same CR 608.2b recheck a spell's targets already get) and an empty result - zero chosen, or every chosen target having since become illegal - resolves doing nothing. `[source]` is used only when the ability's text names no target of its own. Illegal-target refusal at activation (Karn targeting himself) was already correct, generically, through `TargetingProfile.check` in `Game.activate_ability` - only the resolution-time fallback was the defect, and the fix is general, not a Karn-specific registry entry | `scenarios/test_type_changing_and_pt_setting_effects.py::test_karns_plus_one_with_no_target_chosen_changes_nothing`, `::test_karns_plus_one_refuses_karn_himself_as_an_illegal_target` |

You gain life equal to the life lost this way.") as one instruction, the life gained read off the real life-total delta rather than a recomputed devotion total | `scenarios/test_devotion_count.py` |

Deliberately **two-or-more items only** - a bare singular subtype ("target Soldier") is left unread on purpose, since a single capitalized word cannot be told apart from a real English noun with the same confidence a multi-item Oxford list gives; Aeronaut Cavalry's "another target Soldier you control" is the pool's own control fixture for that boundary (`scenarios/test_unparsed_trigger_targets.py`) | `scenarios/test_subtype_restricted_targeting.py` |
| CR 603.6c's generic "leaves the battlefield" trigger condition, and CR 603.10a's self-referential "it" on a dies trigger | CR 603.6c, 603.10a, 700.4 | `TriggerEvent.LEAVES_THE_BATTLEFIELD` (`machines/abilities.py`) is emitted alongside whichever of `DIES`/`LEAVES_BATTLEFIELD_TO_EXILE`/`LEAVES_BATTLEFIELD_TO_HAND` actually happened (`Game.put_into_graveyard`/`exile_permanent`/`return_to_hand`), so a card watching the bare departure (Thought-Knot Seer) fires on any of the three, not only the one it happens to be demonstrated with. `_parse_exile_dead_self_clause`/`_parse_return_dead_self_to_hand_clause` in `machines/effects.py` read "exile it"/"return it to its owner's hand" off a dies trigger's own look-back "it" (Ancient Adamantoise, Rancor) - `_SELF_PHRASES` in `machines/triggers.py` also gained "this aura" (CR 205.3h), missing before and needed for Rancor's own "this Aura is put into a graveyard from the battlefield" condition to be read as self-referential at all | `scenarios/test_trigger_events.py`, `scenarios/test_dies_trigger_self_reference.py`, `scenarios/test_reveal_hand_and_choose.py` |
| CR 103.8's starting player, and a resolving trigger substituting one instruction for another | CR 103.8, 608.2d, 611.2a | `TurnMachine.starting_player` (`machines/turn.py`) reads `players[0]`, fixed for the whole game. `_parse_scry_or_untap_if_not_starting_player_clauses` in `machines/effects.py` reads Forsaken Crossroads' "Scry 1. If you weren't the starting player, you may untap Forsaken Crossroads instead." as one instruction - the second sentence's "instead" replaces the first rather than adding to it, and CR 608.2d's impossible option is never asked of the starting player at all | `scenarios/test_starting_player_conditional_untap.py` |
| The intervening *if* "if you cast it" (CR 601.2's cast, as a defined action) | CR 603.4, 608.2a, 601.2, 611.2a | `intervening_if_holds` in `machines/triggers.py` reads "you cast it" off the same self-referential `CastRecord` lookup the mana-spent/kicker clauses already use (`_cast_record_for_clause`) - unlike those, a missing record is the legitimate, expected answer for a permanent that reached the battlefield some other way, not an unreadable shape to fail closed on, so this returns `False` directly. `_parse_counter_exile_then_cast_free_clauses` reads Transcendent Dragon's whole trigger - the ability-side counterpart of Force of Negation's own counter-then-exile pair - plus a "you may cast it without paying its mana cost" permission with no stated duration (`until_end_of_turn=False`, CR 611.2a), granted to the *ability's controller*, not the countered spell's owner | `scenarios/test_cast_condition_counter_exile_recast.py` |
| A double-faced permanent's *mana ability* (and its own ETB replacement choice) following the face that's up | CR 712.8d, 712.8e, 712.8f, 109.3, 106.12, 305.6 | **Fixed defect, real-game report:** Witch Enchanter // Witch-Blessed Meadow, on the battlefield front face up (a 2/2 creature, no mana ability of its own), was tapped as a mana source for Starfield Shepherd anyway - the log's `mana_sources` shows it going from untapped to tapped in that cast's payment. Cause: `machines/abilities.parse_mana_abilities`, `intrinsic_land_ability`, and `machines/cost.free_tap_mana_abilities`/`free_tap_mana_ability` all read `parse_abilities(card)` (front face *and* every back face) with no face filter at all, unlike `activated_abilities_of`/`triggers_for`, which already followed `CardState.up_face_name`. All four now take an optional `face` parameter with the same "`None` filters nothing" convention, and every caller in `machines/cost.py` (`_auto_tap_candidates`, `_withheld_mana_sources`, `restriction_for_production`) and `tests/scenarios/harness.mana_from` passes `source.up_face_name`. `intrinsic_land_ability` (CR 305.6's unwritten basic-land ability) was reading `Card.types`/`.subtypes` - front face only, structurally - regardless of `face`, which would have misjudged a basic land type printed on a *back* face; it now reads the named face's own type line via a new `_type_line_and_name_for_face` helper. **A second call path was worse than mis-filtered: unvalidated.** `CostPayment.tap_for_mana` - the one place an explicit, hand-picked source list (a scenario's `pay_with`, a player's own `--sources`, the API's `mana_sources`) reaches the pool - trusted its caller completely and never checked the source had a matching mana ability at all; it now re-derives the source's up-face mana abilities and refuses a source/production pair that face cannot actually make. **A related symptom, same root shape:** `machines/replacement._self_enters_modifiers` read `parse_replacement_effects(card_state.card)` unfiltered too, so casting Witch Enchanter's *creature* front face asked Witch-Blessed Meadow's back-face-only "As this land enters, you may pay 3 life" choice; it now filters to `effect.face in (None, card_state.up_face_name)`, the same convention. Two existing `tests/test_cost.py` unit tests had used a mana-less hasty creature (`Raging Goblin`) or a single land tapped for a colour/amount it cannot actually produce as a generic "any tappable thing" stand-in for `CostPayment`'s own bookkeeping, which the new validation correctly refuses; they were changed to real sources that can produce what they're asked for (`Ruby, Daring Tracker`'s `{T}: Add {R} or {G}.`, an extra real Forest/Mountain) rather than the mechanic being loosened back open | `scenarios/test_mdfc_mana_source_face.py` |
| Paying a cost by tapping or exiling objects rather than by spending mana: convoke, delve, improvise, waterbend, and crew | CR 702.51a, 702.51b, 702.66a, 702.66b, 702.122a, 702.122b, 702.126a, 702.126b, 702.126c, 701.67a, 301.7, 301.7a, 301.7b, 601.2f, 601.2h, 733.1, 302.6 | One subsystem, not five special cases. `machines/objects_as_costs.py`'s `ObjectOffer` answers the shared question - *which* objects a keyword lets a player spend, in which zone, and whether they are tapped or exiled - and each keyword supplies its own accounting on top. **Convoke** and **delve** settle per symbol: `CostPayment.substitute` records a `CostSubstitution` (the object, the `PaymentOption` it covers, and the `CostComponent` that spends it), and `CostPayment.mana_owed` is `cost` with those symbols taken out - the pool is asked for `mana_owed`, while `cost` still reads as the real total cost, because CR 702.51b and CR 702.66b both say in as many words that neither keyword is a cost reduction or an alternative cost and that both apply *after* the total cost is determined. Convoke reaches a coloured symbol only with a creature of that colour and any generic symbol with any creature (CR 702.51a); delve reaches generic mana only, so Treasure Cruise's `{U}` still needs a blue source however full the graveyard is. The tapping and exiling happen in CR 601.2h's payment step as ordinary cost components, so CR 733.1's rewind untaps and un-exiles them for free. **Crew** settles against one aggregate threshold instead: `machines/keywords.crew_activation` builds a `KeywordActivation` (the same shape equip and cycling already use) whose cost is the tapping of the chosen creatures, refuses the activation outright unless their total power - read off the computed board, CR 613.5, so a pumped 1/1 counts for more - reaches N, and whose effect adds artifact and creature in layer 4 for `Duration.UNTIL_END_OF_TURN` (CR 514.2/703.4p's cleanup, not the end step). No layer 7 effect is created, because CR 301.7a/301.7b already give a Vehicle its printed P/T the moment it becomes a creature. Crew has no `{T}` symbol in its cost, so CR 302.6/602.5a bind neither the Vehicle nor its crew - a Vehicle played this turn can be crewed by a creature summoned this turn, and only CR 508.1a stops it attacking. The keyword lines themselves (`Convoke (...)`, `Delve (...)`, `Crew N (...)`, `Improvise (...)`) are stripped from effect text by `machines/effects._strip_keyword_ability_lines`, which recognises any keyword line by shape - without it Treasure Cruise's reminder text poisoned "Draw three cards." and the spell resolved doing nothing. **Improvise** (CR 702.126a, "for each generic mana in this spell's total cost, you may tap an untapped artifact you control rather than pay that mana") and **waterbend** (CR 701.67a, the same sentence widened to "artifact **or** creature") are the same per-symbol settlement as convoke and delve, differing only in `ObjectOffer.permitted_types`: `improvise`/`waterbend` in `machines/objects_as_costs.py` are the entry points, and `improvise_payment(card)` answers whether a printing makes the offer at all (CR 702.126c makes multiple instances redundant, so it is a yes/no). Both are generic-only - no "of that color" half - so Metallic Rebuke's `{U}` and Kappa Cannoneer's `{U}` always come from mana however many artifacts are untapped, and CR 702.126b keeps `payment.cost` reading `{5}{U}` while five artifacts pay five of its symbols. Neither uses the `{T}` symbol, so CR 302.6 binds neither: an artifact creature that entered this turn improvises. **One thing is still missing.** Improvise's own line is stripped now (the general keyword-line rule above), so `effect_for` reads Metallic Rebuke and Whir of Invention and Metallic Rebuke counters end to end. Waterbend's printed cost phrase is now read: `machines/cost.waterbend_mana_cost` turns `Waterbend {3}` into the `{3}` CR 701.67a's "Pay [cost]" names, and `total_cost_for_phrase` folds it in. The branch this row used to name as missing is built: `Game.activation_cost`'s `CostKind.OTHER` arm falls back to `total_cost_for_phrase` when `cost_component_for_phrase` returns `None`, because a `CostComponent` cannot carry mana, and folds the mana and the CR 701.67b tap permission back in. `scenarios/test_waterbend.py` covers it end to end and holds no `xfail`. (`judge/test_judge_costs.py`'s own module docstring still says DDR#991 is `xfail`; it is not - the file carries no `xfail` marker) | `scenarios/test_crew.py`, `scenarios/test_convoke.py`, `scenarios/test_delve.py`, `scenarios/test_improvise.py`, `scenarios/test_waterbend.py` |
| CR 603.8 state triggers, and the latch that stops Dark Depths looping | CR 603.8, 704.1a, 603.2, 603.3, 701.6a | `machines/triggers.state_triggers_for` sweeps the battlefield wherever triggers are flushed before priority (`Game.emit`, `Game.put_triggers_on_stack`) for a "there are N or more \<kind\> counters on \<this permanent\>" condition, read off the *computed* ability text so a permanent that lost its abilities is not watching anything. CR 704.1a is why it lives here and not among the state-based actions. `Game.state_trigger_latches` is CR 603.8's "doesn't trigger again until it has left the stack", keyed by source and condition and opened the moment the ability is neither on the stack nor pending - by resolving *or* by being countered (CR 701.6a), which is DDR#961's whole ruling. The Millennium Calendar is the only printing, and its own effect is read now - `_parse_sacrifice_self_clause` (CR 701.21a's "sacrifice it", aimed at the ability's own source) and `parse_clause_effect`'s "each opponent loses 1,000 life", whose count `_COUNT` now reads past its digit-group comma. **Still missing, in `models/game.py`**: `Game._effect_of` is gated on `and ability.ability.event is not None`, and a state trigger has no `TriggerEvent`, so it never asks `trigger_effect_for` for that effect at all | `judge/test_judge_state_triggers.py` |
| CR 603.2d: an ability that triggers an additional time | CR 603.2d, 613.5 | `machines/triggers.additional_trigger_times`, applied inside `triggers_for`: every permanent is asked whether one of its computed ability lines is a trigger multiplier, its own gate is evaluated (the ability's source must be a permanent *its* controller controls, and the event must be the entry the sentence names), and N-1 further `AbilityOnStack` copies are appended. Panharmonicon is the only printing. Only what `triggers_for` builds from a printed ability is multiplied, so a *delayed* triggered ability (CR 603.7a, created by a resolving ability rather than triggered by the event) is untouched for free - DDR#981's point exactly. Not a `LAYER_EFFECTS` row: CR 603.2d is about how many times an ability triggers, not about any object's characteristics | `judge/test_judge_triggers.py` |
| The ante zone (CR 400.1's optional eighth) | CR 400.1, 400.2, 407.3, 407.4, 800.4n | `Zone.ANTE`, shared and public, registered in `Game.shared_zones`. `Game.ante(card_state, player)` is CR 407.4's verb - "To ante an object is to put that object into the ante zone from whichever zone it's currently in. The owner of an object is the only player who can ante that object" - and it answers a boolean, because Jeweled Bird's "Ante this artifact. **If you do**, ..." reads exactly that refusal. `_player_left` skips the ante zone (CR 800.4n). CR 407.3 is why nothing else can put a card there. Jeweled Bird's whole ability is read off its printed text by `machines/effects._parse_ante_self_if_you_do_clause` - the ante, the "if you do" sweep of the owner's other ante cards into their graveyard, and the draw - with no card-name row, so a Bird an opponent has stolen antes nothing and does nothing else either | `judge/test_judge_ante.py` |
| CR 724.1: ending the turn | CR 724.1, 724.1a, 724.1b, 724.1d, 724.1f, 608.2m | `Game.end_the_turn`: CR 724.1a discards the triggers that had not reached the stack, CR 724.1b exiles every object on the stack *including the one resolving* (`Game.resolving`, tracked by `resolve_top`, with `Game.turn_ended_exiled_ids` keeping `resolve_top` from overwriting that zone afterwards - CR 608.2m has the spell finish resolving anyway), CR 724.1d removes everything from combat and jumps the turn machine to `Step.CLEANUP` without running the cleanup step's own turn-based actions, and CR 724.1f gives nobody priority. **CR 724.1c and CR 724.1e could not be confirmed** with `knowledge_search(type="rules")` and are neither implemented nor cited. Time Stop's "End the turn." has no `machines/effects.py` row, so nothing calls this from a resolution yet | `judge/test_judge_ending_the_turn.py` |
| Triggered mana abilities, resolved inline, and CR 104.4b's one detectable loop | CR 605.1b, 605.4a, 605.5, 104.4b, 106.12 | `Game.resolve_triggered_mana_abilities` resolves a CR 605.1b triggered mana ability the moment it triggers, off the stack, exactly as CR 605.4a requires ("it simply resolves immediately after the mana ability that triggered it, without waiting for priority"). A new `ExtraTriggerEvent.MANA_ADDED` (`machines/triggers.mana_added_event`, with `GameEvent.mana_added` carrying the colours) is the "an ability caused you to add mana" event, deliberately distinct from CR 106.12's "tapped for mana": Caged Sun's "Whenever a land's ability causes you to add one or more mana of the chosen color" asks about the adding. Each inline resolution that adds mana re-raises that event, and the chain is bounded by `Game.TRIGGERED_MANA_ABILITY_LOOP_BOUND`, past which `Game.drawn` is set (CR 104.4b). **This is the only loop shape detected anywhere** - see `Game.drawn`'s docstring. `machines/cost.CostPayment.tap_for_mana` still emits only the tap event and does not resolve inline, so the two mana paths disagree until it is updated. Caged Sun's own "add an additional one mana of that color" is read now by `_parse_mana_adding_trigger`, whose "one mana of the chosen color" shape was widened to CR 607.1's anaphoric "that color" (the same CR 614.12a choice on `CardState.choice`). **Still missing, in `models/game.py`**: the same `and ability.ability.event is not None` gate on `Game._effect_of` - Caged Sun's condition has no `TriggerEvent` either, so the effect is never looked up | `judge/test_judge_mana_abilities.py` |
| `Keyword X, where X is ...`, a keyword whose parameter the card defines in its own words | CR 702.189a, 608.2h | `machines/abilities._parse_keyword_parameter`, tried before the full-stop test that would disqualify the line and before the comma split that would break it in two. Firebending Student's "Firebending X, where X is this creature's power." now parses as a `KeywordAbility` (canonical `Firebending`, `parameter` kept verbatim) instead of a `StaticAbility`. `count` is deliberately left None: CR 608.2h reads the number "only once, when the effect is applied", which is as the firebending ability resolves. The add-mana effect is built now - `machines/effects._parse_firebending_add_mana` reads "add X {R}, where X is this creature's power. Until end of combat, you don't lose this mana as steps and phases end" and reads the power on resolution (CR 608.2h). Still missing, elsewhere: `machines/keywords._TRIGGER_EXPANSIONS` has no `firebending` row, so CR 702.189a's triggered ability is never built at all; and the second sentence is matched but not carried out, since keeping mana through a step ending is a replacement effect on CR 500.5/703.4q that `Game.empty_mana_pools` already records as unmodelled | `judge/test_judge_mana_abilities.py` |
| "Exile any number of other target creatures ... until this permanent leaves the battlefield" with a linked delayed counter trigger | CR 610.3, 603.7a, 603.7b, 603.7c, 603.7d, 107.1c, 608.2b | `machines/effects._parse_exile_targets_until_leaves_then_counters` reads Lagrella, the Magpie's whole ability as one CR 608.2c instruction. Every legal target is exiled (CR 107.1c: "any number" may be zero), each with its own CR 610.3 return pair through the same `_watch_for_departure_and_return_exiled_card` Static Prison uses, plus a second `DelayedTrigger` per exiled card for "when an exiled card enters under your control this way, put two +1/+1 counters on it" - a separate ability, so it goes on the stack when the card comes back and can be responded to there. Panharmonicon doubling the *enters* ability (CR 603.2d) doubles only the exile: the second copy finds its target already gone and does not resolve at all (CR 608.2b) | `judge/test_judge_triggers.py` |
| A continuous effect whose magnitude is an X the ability defines mid-sentence | CR 608.2h, 611.2c, 613.1d, 613.1e, 613.1f, 613.4b, 402.1 | `machines/effects._parse_where_x_is_continuous_effect` lifts ", where X is \<expression>" out of *any* sentence rather than only off the end of a mass pump, resolves it once as the ability resolves (CR 608.2h), and hands the resulting literal to `machines/layers.parsed_layer_effects`. Jolrael, Voice of Zhalfir's "up to one target land you control becomes an X/X green and blue Bird creature with flying and haste until end of turn, where X is the number of cards in your hand. It's still a land." is the printing that needs it - the tail sits in the middle of the ability, so the end-anchored `_WHERE_X_IS_RE` never saw it. `count_expression` learned "the number of cards in your hand" (CR 402.1: every card in the zone, with no type to filter on) | `judge/test_judge_copying.py` |
| "Untap target creature and gain control of it until end of turn." - a one-shot and a layer 2 effect in one sentence | CR 701.26b, 613.1b, 611.2c, 607.1 | `machines/effects._parse_untap_and_gain_control_clause` (Threaten, Act of Treason and that template). Read whole, because `split_effect_clauses` cuts at the bare "and" and leaves a control clause whose object is a pronoun; the untap is applied here and the two continuous halves are rewritten into the sentences `machines/layers.py` already reads on their own ("gain control of target creature until end of turn.", "target creature gains haste until end of turn.") and built against the targets the spell actually chose. The layer 2 effect is visible everywhere now: `CardState.stored_controller` is the base and `CardState.controller` is a property consulting the computed board (CR 613.5), which is what `Game.controller_of`, combat, the state-based actions and a delayed trigger all read. `ContinuousEffects.has_control_effect` short-circuits that lookup when no control effect is in play, since `set_controller` is reachable from one builder only | `judge/test_judge_bending.py` |


## Can you actually play a game?

A game starts and runs now: `Match.start_game` loads each registered deck
(`Player.load_deck`, with the sideboard set aside for games two and three -
CR 103.2a), shuffles it seeded (CR 103.3), sets life to 20 (CR 103.4), deals
opening hands of seven (CR 103.5), and then leaves the game *resting* in
`Step.MULLIGAN` - the first of two pregame steps that are not CR 500 steps of a
turn (CR 500.1) and say so: no phase, `turn_number` and `turns_taken` at 0, no
priority. Seats declare through `Game.declare_mulligan` and take CR 103.6's
actions through `Game.take_opening_hand_action` /
`Game.finish_opening_hand_actions`, none of which asks anybody anything, so a
live seat never blocks the engine. `Game.settle_pregame` - which
`Match.run_mulligans` is a thin wrapper over - is the headless path and settles
both sequences through the ordinary decision defaults in one call. Who goes
first is decided by CR 103.1 - randomly,
by name, or by the loser of the previous game through `Match.chooser_for_next_game`.
`scenarios/test_starting_a_game.py`, `scenarios/test_mulligans.py`,
`scenarios/test_pregame_steps.py`, and
`scenarios/test_opening_hand_actions.py` play it
through with real tournament decklists. From there, `Game(automatic_turn_based_actions=True)`
drives the whole turn: untap, draw, the combat declarations through the decision
machinery, cleanup - a creature attack declared by its player, blocked by the
defender, and dealt damage, all from `advance()` (`scenarios/test_combat_turn_based_actions.py`).

What still separates this from a game two people could actually play:

1. **The card effects registry is the bottleneck.** `machines/effects.py` is a
   hand-written registry keyed by card name; every card without an entry
   resolves as a no-op, which is most of them. Casting, targeting, paying, and
   the stack all work - it is the resolution that does nothing. **A slice of
   this is now closed**: `parse_player_effect`/`parse_player_effect_for_spell`
   read a single-clause, no-target, no-choice player effect - life gain and
   loss, energy, drawing, milling, surveil, scry, and any straight-line
   combination of those joined by "and"/"then"/a sentence boundary (CR
   608.2c) - straight off a card's own printed text, tried on a registry miss
   in all three resolution paths (`effect_for`, `trigger_effect_for`,
   `activated_effect_for`). It fails **closed**: one clause it cannot read
   (a target, a modal choice) throws the whole effect away rather than
   running the part it understood. **"You may `<clause>`." is now read
   too** (CR 608.2d), for both the player-effect wave above and the
   targeted wave (destroy/exile/return/tap/untap/counters/damage-target,
   `parse_targeted_effect`/`parse_targeted_effect_for_spell`) - wrapping is
   only ever applied over a clause the parser already understands, so "you
   may `<something unread>`" still throws the whole effect away rather than
   asking a question it could not carry out. "`<A>`. If you do, `<B>`." (CR
   118.12) is now read for the **sacrifice** shape: "you may sacrifice this
   `<type>`" (Sanctum of Ugin - the permanent bearing the ability itself,
   never a choice among several) and "you may sacrifice another `<type>`"
   (Disciple of Freyalise - a real CR 608.2d choice among whatever the
   player controls of that type), each paired with its own "if you do"
   consequence - a plain mandatory search (`_search_and_place_for`, run with
   `optional=False` since CR 118.12's own "you may" already gated it) or a
   magnitude-threaded pair of clauses reading "X" as the sacrificed
   permanent's power, captured before `Game.sacrifice` moves it to the
   graveyard the same CR 608.2i way `_parse_anaphoric_clause_pair` already
   does. CR 118.12's own text is the reason the consequence never re-checks
   the board afterward: "the 'If \[a player\] does' clause checks whether
   the player chose to pay ... regardless of what events actually
   occurred" - CR 701.21a's sacrifice has no failure mode for a permanent
   the player controls, so "did you do it" is only ever "did you say yes".
   `machines/effects._parse_conditional_sacrifice_clause`,
   `ConditionalClauseNotice`; `scenarios/test_conditional_sacrifice_clauses.py`.
   Still not implemented: the **pay-life** cousin of the same CR 118.12 shape -
   Necrodominance's "you may pay any amount of life. If you do, draw that many
   cards." It once needed a decision primitive this engine did not have -
   "choose an amount of life to pay" is not `PAY_LIFE`'s fixed `amount`, and
   no `DecisionKind` read a variable answer back as an integer. That primitive
   exists now: `DecisionKind.PAY_ENERGY_AMOUNT` and `Game.pay_any_amount_of_
   energy` (see the energy row above), built for Galvanic Discharge's and
   Wrath of the Skies's own "you may pay any amount of {E}" rather than for
   Necrodominance - its own clause is still unwired, but the missing piece was
   the shape of the question (an integer, not a boolean), which is no longer
   a gap in the machinery, only in which cards call it. **Turntimber
   Symbiosis's own clause is fixed (W13)** - see its own row below in "Cards
   from the metagame" - and turned out not to need this primitive at all: "you
   may put a creature card ... onto the battlefield. If that card has mana
   value 3 or less, ..." is a plain CR 608.2d `MAY`/`CHOOSE_CARDS` choice with
   a check on the chosen card's own mana value read inline, not an amount
   fed back as an integer. Targeted effects with a restriction the
   parser cannot enforce, other choices, and token creation are still the
   registry's job.
2. **Opening-hand actions are modelled; CR 103.5b's are not.** CR 103.6's
   sequence is a step the game rests in (`Step.PREGAME_ACTIONS`), driven either
   seat by seat through `Game.take_opening_hand_action` /
   `Game.finish_opening_hand_actions` or in one call by
   `Game.take_opening_hand_actions`, over `machines/opening_hand.py` - both
   subrules, in CR 103.6's own order (starting player first, then the rest).
   CR 103.6a's begin-on-the-battlefield action covers the four Leylines in the
   pool and Gemstone Caverns, including its "you're not the starting player"
   restriction (CR 103.8), its luck counter, and its "exile a card from your
   hand" rider; CR 103.6b's reveal covers Devourer of Destiny, including the
   delayed triggered ability (CR 603.7) its reveal creates for the revealer's
   first upkeep. Both are asked as a `DecisionKind.OPENING_HAND_ACTION`
   question and declined by an unscripted player. See
   `scenarios/test_opening_hand_actions.py`. **What is left**: CR 103.5b's "any
   time [that player] could mulligan" effects, and the Chancellor cycle - no
   Chancellor is in the fixture pool, so its "when each opponent casts their
   first spell of the game" rider has nothing to test against and no `RIDERS`
   entry. A card that prints an opening-hand paragraph the reader cannot match,
   or one that matches but carries a rider with no `RIDERS` entry, is refused
   the action outright and recorded on `Game.opening_hand_notices` rather than
   half-performed.
3. **Decisions default rather than ask.** Unscripted questions - a mulligan,
